trivialise. We now define a cofinal system of strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}
inside
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Definition 3.4.1
.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer. We say that a quasi-compact open affinoid subspace
\mathcal{U}\subset P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
is an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
if

(1)

One has
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}=\mathcal{U}\cap P_{\operatorname{dR},A_{\operatorname{ord}}}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

(2)

\mathcal{U}
contains the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}
inside
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Given an overconvergent extension
\mathcal{U}
, we set
\mathcal{U}_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{U}\cap P_{\operatorname{dR},A_{r}}^{\operatorname{an}}
for any integer
r\geq 1
. Note that any
\mathcal{U}_{r}
is also an overconvergent extension.

Proposition 3.4.2
.

For any
n\geq 1
, there exists an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
. Moreover, the collection of overconvergent extensions of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
for varying
n
forms a cofinal system of quasi-compact open strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}
inside
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 1
. Then
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n+1}=\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n+1,n+1}
(resp.
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}=\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n+1,n}
) is described by the inequalities

\displaystyle|P_{\alpha_{n+1}}(T_{1})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n+1}}|,\quad|P_{\gamma_{n+1}}(T_{2})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n+1}}|,\quad|Q_{n+1}(T_{1},U)|\leq|p^{n+1}|

\displaystyle\text{(resp. }|P_{\alpha_{n+1}}(T_{1})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n}}|,\quad|P_{\gamma_{n+1}}(T_{2})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n}}|,\quad|Q_{n+1}(T_{1},U)|\leq|p^{n}|\text{ ).}

Since
A_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}=A^{+}\langle 1/h\rangle
, there exists a sufficiently large integer
N\geq 0
, and elements
P_{\alpha_{n+1}}^{\prime}(T_{1})\in A^{+}[T_{1}]
,
P_{\gamma_{n+1}}^{\prime}(T_{2})\in A^{+}[T_{2}]
,
Q_{n+1}^{\prime}(T_{1},U)\in A^{+}[T_{1},U]
such that

P^{\prime}_{\alpha_{n+1}}(T_{1})\equiv h^{N}P_{\alpha_{n+1}}(T_{1}),\quad P^{\prime}_{\gamma_{n+1}}(T_{2})\equiv h^{N}P_{\gamma_{n+1}}(T_{2}),\quad Q_{n+1}^{\prime}(T_{1},U)\equiv h^{N}Q_{n+1}(T_{1},U)

modulo
p^{t}A_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
, for some fixed integer
t
such that
p^{-t}\leq\rho_{n+1,n+1}
.

Define
\mathcal{V}
(resp.
\mathcal{U}
) to be the rational subset of
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
defined by the inequalities

\displaystyle|P^{\prime}_{\alpha_{n+1}}(T_{1})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n+1}}|,\quad|P^{\prime}_{\gamma_{n+1}}(T_{2})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n+1}}|,\quad|Q^{\prime}_{n+1}(T_{1},U)|\leq|p^{n+1}|

\displaystyle\text{(resp. }|P^{\prime}_{\alpha_{n+1}}(T_{1})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n}}|,\quad|P^{\prime}_{\gamma_{n+1}}(T_{2})|\leq|p^{\nu_{n+1,n}}|,\quad|Q^{\prime}_{n+1}(T_{1},U)|\leq|p^{n}|\text{ ).}

Then
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}\subset\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{U}
. Since
|p^{\nu_{n+1,n+1}}|<|p^{\nu_{n+1,n}}|
(Lemma
3.2.1
) and
|p^{n+1}|<|p^{n}|
, we see that
\overline{\mathcal{V}}\subset\mathcal{U}
, where the closure is in
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
. This implies that
\mathcal{U}
is an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
. The rest of the proposition follows from a standard compactness argument, and the fact that
\{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}:n\geq 1\}
are cofinal over the ordinary locus.
∎

Let
\mathcal{U}
be an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
for some integer
n\geq 1
. Then we have a chain of quasi-compact open affinoid neighbourhoods

\mathcal{U}_{1}\supset\mathcal{U}_{2}\supset\ldots\supset\mathcal{U}_{r}\supset\ldots\supset\mathcal{U}_{\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}

such that
\mathcal{U}_{\infty}
is the locus in
\mathcal{U}_{r}
(
\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{U}\cap P_{\operatorname{dR},A_{r}}^{\operatorname{an}}
) where the Hasse invariant
h\in\mathcal{O}^{+}(\mathcal{U}_{r})
is invertible, for any
r\geq 1
. On sections, this chain of inclusions is induced by maps

B_{1}^{+}\to B_{2}^{+}\to\ldots\to B_{\infty}^{+}

where
B_{r}^{+}=\mathcal{O}^{+}(\mathcal{U}_{r})
. Each
B_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{r})
is a Banach space – denote the norm for which
B_{r}^{+}
is the unit ball by
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{r}
. The key result of this section is to show that these morphisms are very close to being isometries, with the failure to being an isometry measured by powers of the Hasse invariant (whose valuation can be made arbitrarily small). This will allow us to overconverge the Gauss–Manin connection to
p
-adic powers.

Proposition 3.4.3
.

Let
r\geq 1
. With notation as above, there exists an integer
M\geq 0
(depending on
r
) such that for any
k\geq 1
, the kernel of the map

B^{+}_{r}/p^{k}\to B^{+}_{\infty}/p^{k}

is killed by
h^{kM}
.

Proof.

We first establish the case
k=1
. Note that
B_{\infty}^{+}=B_{r}^{+}\langle 1/h\rangle
because
\mathcal{U}_{r}
is an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\infty}
. This implies that
(B_{\infty}^{+}/p)=(B_{r}^{+}/p)[1/h]
, and hence any element
x\in B_{r}^{+}/p
in the kernel of the map
B_{r}^{+}/p\to B_{\infty}^{+}/p
is killed by some power of the Hasse invariant. Since
B_{r}^{+}/p
is Noetherian, the kernel of this map is a finitely-generated ideal, and hence we can find a sufficiently large integer
M\geq 0
such that
h^{M}
kills the kernel.

This proves the proposition in the case
k=1
. The general case now follows from a simple induction argument using the fact that
B_{\infty}^{+}
is
p
-torsion free.
∎

The following corollary is a generalisation of
[
AI21
, Proposition 4.10]
and it will be key for the proof of our main result.

Corollary 3.4.4
.

With notation as above, for any real number
0<\delta<1
there exists an integer
s=s(\delta)\geq r
such that the following holds: for all
k\geq 1
,
x\in B_{r}
and
c\in\mathbb{Q}
, one has

|\!|x|\!|_{\infty}\leq p^{c-k}\text{ and }|\!|x|\!|_{r}\leq p^{c}\quad\Rightarrow\quad|\!|x|\!|_{s}\leq p^{c-\delta k}.

Proof.

By raising
x
to an integral power, it is enough to prove the statement for
c\in\mathbb{Z}
, and by rescaling it is enough to prove the statement for
c=0
. Therefore, we have an element
x\in B_{r}^{+}
whose image is in
p^{k}B_{\infty}^{+}
. By Proposition
3.4.3
, there exists an
M\geq 0
such that
|\!|(p^{-1}h^{M})^{k}x|\!|_{s}\leq|\!|(p^{-1}h^{M})^{k}x|\!|_{r}\leq 1
for any
s\geq r
and any
k\geq 1
. Since
|\!|h^{-1}|\!|_{s}\to 1^{+}
as
s\to+\infty
, taking
s
large enough such that
|\!|h^{-1}|\!|_{s}\leq p^{\frac{(1-\delta)}{M}}
we obtain

|\!|x|\!|_{s}\leq|\!|(ph^{-M})^{k}|\!|_{s}\leq p^{-\delta k}

as required.
∎

4.
p
-adic interpolation of continuous operators

In this section we establish the general abstract results on
p
-adic interpolation of operators that are used in §
5
in order to
p
-adically interpolate the Gauss-Manin connection. It is likely that the results in this section can be generalised to the setting of PEL Shimura varieties – in particular some of the notation in this section will differ from the rest of the article.

Remark 4.0.1
.

At this point it might be useful to recall the strategy we described in the introduction for showing the
p
-adic interpolation of the Gauss--Manin connection. We make some comments concerning how the abstract results will be used in the following section.
3
3

3

The reader can also choose to read §
5
before reading this section.

(1)

In §
4.2
we introduce general definitions of continuous, analytic and locally analytic actions on topological modules over
R[1/p]
for some admissible
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
-algebra
R
. Proposition
4.2.4
will be helpful as we will show that the Gauss-Manin connection has locally a very simple description modulo large powers of
p
(cf. Proposition
5.2.1
).

(2)

In §
4.3
we will specialise to a more precise situation and the rings
L_{m}=S^{+}\langle\frac{X-1}{p^{m}},\frac{Y}{p^{m}},\frac{Z-1}{p^{m}}\rangle
will correspond by Proposition
5.2.1
to (the ring of functions of) one of the balls whose disjoint union forms the strict quasi-compact open neighbourhoods
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},m}
of the Igusa tower over the ordinary locus of Corollary
3.3.3
.

(3)

Finally, the sequence
V_{0}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq V_{r}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq V_{\infty}
considered in §
4.4
will correspond (locally) to a collection of overconvergent extensions of the neighbourhoods
\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{HT},m}
.

4.1.
Function spaces

Let
R^{+}
be an admissible
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
-algebra and set
R=R^{+}[1/p]
. Let
C_{\operatorname{cont}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
,
C^{h\operatorname{-an}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
and
C^{\operatorname{la}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
denote the
R
-algebras of continuous, analytic of radius
p^{-h}
(
h\in{\mathbb{N}}
), and locally analytic functions from
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
to
R
respectively. We also set
C_{\operatorname{cont}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R^{+})
to be the
R^{+}
-algebra of continuous functions from
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
to
R^{+}
. We recall the following classical result.

Proposition 4.1.1

(Amice)
.

For
k\geq 0
set
\binom{x}{k}=\frac{x(x-1)\cdots(x-k+1)}{k!}
. Then

•

The family
\binom{x}{k}
is an orthonormal basis of
C_{\operatorname{cont}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
(over
R
).

•

The family
\lfloor\frac{k}{p^{h}}\rfloor!\binom{x}{k}
is an orthonormal basis of
C^{h\operatorname{-an}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
.

•

A function
f=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}a_{k}\binom{x}{k}\in C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
is locally analytic if and only if for some
\varepsilon>0
, the term
p^{k\varepsilon}|a_{k}|\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
, where
|\cdot|
denotes the Banach norm on
R
with unit ball
R^{+}
.

Proof.

See
[
Col10
, Corolaire I.2.4, Théorème I.4.7, Corolaire I.4.8]
.
∎

For any
\varepsilon>0
we will denote by
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
the subspace of functions
f=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}a_{k}\binom{x}{k}
with
p^{k\varepsilon}|a_{k}|\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
. Observe that
C^{\operatorname{la}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)=\varinjlim_{h\to+\infty}C^{h\operatorname{-an}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)=\varinjlim_{\varepsilon>0}C_{\varepsilon}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
.

4.2.
Continuous and analytic actions

We begin with some elementary calculations. Let
R^{+}
be an admissible
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra,
R=R^{+}[1/p]
and let
V
be a
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-Banach vector space equipped with a topological
R
-module structure.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
C=C_{\operatorname{cont}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
or
C_{\varepsilon}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
or
C^{\operatorname{la}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
and let
W
be a topological
R
-module. If
T\in\mathrm{End}_{R}(W)
is a continuous operator, then we say that
T
extends to a continuous (resp.
\varepsilon
-analytic, resp. locally analytic) action if there exists a continuous
R
-algebra action of
C_{\operatorname{cont}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
(resp.
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
, resp.
C^{\operatorname{la}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
), i.e. a continuous
R
-bilinear map

C\times W\to W,

(where the left hand side is equipped with the product topology) that respects the algebra structure on
C
given by multiplication of functions, such that the structural map
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\to R
acts as
T
.

Lemma 4.2.2
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{End}_{R}(V)
be a continuous operator and let
|\!|\cdot|\!|
denote the operator norm on
\mathrm{End}_{R}(V)
. Then

(1)

T
extends to a continuous action if and only if the norms

\left|\!\left|\frac{T(T-1)\ldots(T-k+1)}{k!}\right|\!\right|

are uniformly bounded for all
k\geq 0
.

(2)

T
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action if and only if the norms

p^{-k\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\frac{T(T-1)\ldots(T-k+1)}{k!}\right|\!\right|

are uniformly bounded for all
k\geq 0
.

(3)

T
extends to a locally analytic action if and only if for all
\varepsilon>0
the norms

p^{-k\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\frac{T(T-1)\ldots(T-k+1)}{k!}\right|\!\right|

are uniformly bounded for all
k\geq 0
.

Moreover, any such extension of
T
is unique.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of the characterisation of continuous,
\varepsilon
-analytic and locally analytic functions in Proposition
4.1.1
.
∎

We will also need the following uniqueness property for
\varepsilon
-analytic/locally analytic actions on
\operatorname{LB}
-spaces with injective transition maps.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

Let
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a (filtered) countable sequence of
R
-modules which are also
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Banach spaces, with each transition map injective. Let
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{i\in I}V_{i}
equipped with the direct limit topology. Let
\varepsilon>0
and let
T_{i}\colon V_{i}\to V_{i}
be continuous
R
-linear operators, all compatible with each other via the transition maps. Set
T=\varinjlim_{i\in I}T_{i}
.

(1)

Let
j\in I
and
C(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)^{\operatorname{pol}}\subset C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
be the subalgebra of polynomial functions. There is a natural
R
-algebra action of
C(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)^{\operatorname{pol}}
on
V_{j}
extending
T_{j}
. Then any continuous
R
-linear action

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times V_{j}\to V

which extends this
R
-algebra action of
C(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)^{\operatorname{pol}}
is unique.

(2)

Any continuous
R
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times V\to V

extending
T
is unique.

(3)

Any continuous
R
-algebra action

C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times V\to V

extending
T
is unique.

Proof.

In this proof we will continually use the fact that if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Banach space and
Y=\varinjlim_{i\in I}Y_{i}
is a countable filtered colimit of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Banach spaces (with the direct limit topology) where all the transition maps are injective, then any continuous
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear map

U\colon X\to\varinjlim_{i\in I}Y_{i}=Y

factors through
Y_{i}
for some
i\in I
(see
[
Sch02
, Corollary 8.9]
). Note that
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Banach space, and
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)=\varinjlim_{\varepsilon>0}C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
with the direct limit topology.

For part (1), let
f\in C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
and
v\in V_{j}
. Then
f
can be written as the limit of
\{f_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
, where
f_{k}\in C(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)^{\operatorname{pol}}
. The map

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\to V,\quad\quad g\mapsto g\cdot v

is continuous (by assumption), so by the above, factors though some
V_{i}
. Since
V_{i}
is Hausdorff, we must have that
f\cdot v
is the (unique) limit of
f_{k}\cdot v
, which are already determined.

Part (2) is similar, using the fact that the action of polynomial functions are already determined by
T
(because it is an algebra action). Part (3) just follows from part (2).
∎

The following useful result explains how one can perturb a locally analytic action to produce an
\varepsilon
-analytic action for some appropriately chosen
\varepsilon
.

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Let
T_{1},T_{2}\in\mathrm{End}_{R}(V)
and suppose that
T_{1}
extends to a locally analytic action and that
|\!|T_{2}|\!|\leq 1
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an integer
N_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
(depending on
T_{1}
but not on
T_{2}
) such that
T_{1}+p^{N_{\varepsilon}}T_{2}
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action.

Proof.

Let
N\geq 0
and denote
T=T_{1}+p^{N}T_{2}
. Let
f_{k}(X)
denote the polynomial

f_{k}(X)=\frac{X(X-1)\ldots(X-k+1)}{k!}.

Let
\Lambda
denote the set of all ordered tuples
I=(-1=t_{0}\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<\ldots<t_{2r-1}\leq t_{2r}=k-1)
with
r\geq 1
. For such an
I\in\Lambda
and any
1\leq i\leq r
, let
k_{i}=t_{2i-1}-t_{2i-2}
and
\ell_{i}=t_{2i}-t_{2i-1}
, and set

z_{I}=f_{k_{1}}(T_{1}-(t_{0}+1))\cdot T_{2}^{\ell_{1}}\cdot f_{k_{2}}(T_{1}-(t_{2}+1))\cdot T_{2}^{\ell_{2}}\cdot\ldots\cdot f_{k_{r}}(T_{1}-(t_{2r}+1))\cdot T_{2}^{\ell_{r}}.

Then we can write

(4.2.5)

f_{k}(T)=f_{k}(T_{1})+\sum_{I\in\Lambda,b\neq 0}\frac{p^{Nb}}{b!}\binom{k}{a}^{-1}\binom{a}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{r}}^{-1}z_{I},

where we have denoted
a=\sum_{i=1}^{r}k_{i}
and
b=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\ell_{i}
so that
a+b=k
.

We now give a bound for the operators
z_{I}
. Note that for any integer
t\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
f_{k}(X-t)
is also a polynomial in
X
and the Mahler expansion of
f_{k}(X-t)
is of the form

f_{k}(X-t)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}a_{i}f_{i}(X)

for
a_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
because
f_{k}(x-t)\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
for any
x\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
and the coefficients are just computed
using the discrete difference operator. This implies that, if
\varepsilon>0
and
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
is such that

p^{-k\varepsilon/2}|\!|f_{k}(T_{1})|\!|\leq C

for all
k\geq 0
then,
for any
t\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
k\geq 0
, we have

p^{-k\varepsilon/2}|\!|f_{k}(T_{1}-t)|\!|\leq C.

Hence, by the fact that
|\!|T_{2}|\!|\leq 1
, we deduce

(4.2.6)

p^{-a\varepsilon/2}|\!|z_{I}|\!|\leq C^{r}.

Using that for any multinomial coefficient we have
v_{p}(\binom{n}{a_{1},\ldots,a_{s}}^{-1})\geq-\log_{p}(n)
, we get from (
4.2.5
) and (
4.2.6
) that

p^{-k\varepsilon}|\!|f_{k}(T)|\!|\leq\mathrm{max}(C,p^{-k\varepsilon}p^{-Nb+b/(p-1)}p^{2\log_{p}(k)}p^{(k-b)\varepsilon/2}C^{r}).

Since
b\geq r-1
, taking
N
such that
p^{-N+1/(p-1)}\leq C^{-1}
we deduce that
p^{-Nb+b/(p-1)}C^{r}\leq C
and the right hand side is bounded above by

p^{-k\varepsilon/2+2\log_{p}(k)}C

which is uniformly bounded in
k
as
\varepsilon>0
. This concludes the proof.
∎

4.3.
Nilpotent operators

Let
R^{+}\to S^{+}
be a morphism of
p
-adically complete and separated,
p
-torsion free
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
-algebras with
R^{+}
admissible, and denote by
R=R^{+}[1/p],S=S^{+}[1/p]
their associated
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-Banach algebras,

Definition 4.3.1
.

Let
L_{0}=S^{+}\langle X,Y,Z\rangle
and, for any integer
m\geq 1
, let
L_{m}=S^{+}\langle\frac{X-1}{p^{m}},\frac{Y}{p^{m}},\frac{Z-1}{p^{m}}\rangle
. Set
V_{0}=L_{0}[1/p]
,
V_{m}=L_{m}[1/p]
.

We equip
V_{0}
with the norm induced by the lattice
L_{0}
, or equivalently the norm given by
|\!|\sum_{a,b,c}s_{a,b,c}X^{a}Y^{b}Z^{c}|\!|=\max_{a,b,c}|\!|s_{a,b,c}|\!|
, where the norm on the right hand side is the
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-Banach norm of
S
. This allows us to view
V_{0}
as a Banach algebra with unit ball
L_{0}
. Analogously, for
m\geq 1
, we can view
V_{m}
as a Banach algebra with the norm induced by the lattice
L_{m}
, or equivalently defined by
|\!|\sum_{a,b,c}s_{a,b,c}(X-1)^{a}Y^{b}(Z-1)^{c}|\!|_{m}=\max_{a,b,c}p^{-m(a+b+c)}|\!|s_{a,b,c}|\!|
.

Let
\theta:S^{+}\to S^{+}
be an
R^{+}
-linear derivation, which can naturally be viewed as an
S^{+}
-linear functional
\Omega^{1}_{S^{+}/R^{+}}\to S^{+}
. We define
\nabla_{\theta}:V_{m}\to V_{m}
to be the unique
R
-linear derivation such that it acts as
\theta
on
S
and satisfies

\nabla_{\theta}(X)=Y,\;\;\;\nabla_{\theta}(Y)=\nabla_{\theta}(Z)=0.

In particular, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

where the vertical maps are induced by sending
X\mapsto 1
,
Y\mapsto 0
,
Z\mapsto 1
.

We will show next that, whenever
\theta:S^{+}\to S^{+}
extends to a continuous action, the action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)\subseteq C_{\operatorname{cont}}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}},R)
on
S
extends to
V_{m}
for any
m\geq 0
. We begin with some elementary lemmas for which we introduce some notation. For any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
and
0\leq r\leq k
, let
\Sigma_{k,r}
be the set of subsets of
\{0,\ldots,k-1\}
of size
r
. If
I\in\Sigma_{k,r}
, let
k_{1},\ldots,k_{\ell}
(with
\ell
and the
k_{i}
’s depending on
I
) be the lengths of the largest blocks of consecutive integers in
I
, so that
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}k_{i}=r
. More precisely,
I
can be written as

I=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\ell}[i_{j},i_{j}+k_{j}-1]=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\ell}I_{j}

with no adjacent intervals, i.e.
i_{j}>i_{j-1}+k_{j-1}
for all
2\leq j\leq\ell
. For any
I\in\Sigma_{k,r}
, we denote

g_{I}(X)=\prod_{i\in I}(X-i),\;\;\;f_{I}(X)=\prod_{1\leq j\leq\ell}\frac{1}{k_{j}!}\prod_{i\in I_{j}}(X-i),

with the convention that
g_{\emptyset}=f_{\emptyset}=1
. The following easy lemma will be very useful.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

We have

f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(sX^{a}Y^{b}Z^{c})=\sum_{r=0}^{\min(k,a)}\sum_{I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}}\binom{k-r}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\ell}}^{-1}\binom{k}{r}^{-1}\binom{a}{r}f_{I}(\theta)(s)X^{a-r}Y^{b+r}Z^{c}.

Proof.

First observe that, since everything is linear with respect to the variable
Z
, we may assume that
c=0
. We have

\binom{k-r}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\ell}}^{-1}\binom{k}{r}^{-1}\binom{a}{r}f_{I}(\theta)(s)=\frac{a(a-1)\cdots(a-r+1)}{k!}g_{I}(\theta)(s),

so that we need to prove the formula

f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(sX^{a}Y^{b})=\sum_{r=0}^{\min(k,a)}\sum_{I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}}\frac{a(a-1)\cdots(a-r+1)}{k!}g_{I}(\theta)(s)X^{a-r}Y^{b+r}.

We check the formula by induction. For
k=1
we need to prove that

f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(sX^{a}Y^{b})=\theta(s)X^{a}Y^{b}+asX^{a-1}Y^{b+1},

which follows from Leibniz rule. Assume the result holds for
k
. We calculate

\displaystyle f_{k+1}(\nabla_{\theta})(sX^{a}Y^{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nabla_{\theta}-k)}{k+1}f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(sX^{a}Y^{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\theta}-k)\sum_{r=0}^{\min(k,a)}\sum_{I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}}\frac{a(a-1)\cdots(a-r+1)}{(k+1)!}g_{I}(\theta)(s)X^{a-r}Y^{b+r}.

We have

(\nabla_{\theta}-k)g_{I}(\theta)(s)X^{a-r}Y^{b+r}=(\theta-k)g_{I}(\theta)(s)X^{a-r}Y^{b+r}+g_{I}(\theta)(s)(a-r)X^{a-(r+1)}Y^{b+(r+1)},

and observe that
(\theta-k)g_{I}(X)=g_{I\cup\{k\}}(X)
. The result follows by decomposing
\Sigma_{k+1,k-r}
as those subsets containing
k
and those not containing
k
.
∎

The following corollary implies that, if the extension of
\nabla_{\theta}
to
V_{m}
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action, then the same holds for any
m^{\prime}\geq m
.

Corollary 4.3.3
.

Let
m^{\prime}\geq m
,
s\in S
,
a,b\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
k\geq 0
. Then

\bigg|\!\bigg|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})\left(s\left(\frac{X-1}{p^{m}}\right)^{a}\left(\frac{Y}{p^{m}}\right)^{b}\left(\frac{Z-1}{p^{m}}\right)^{c}\right)\bigg|\!\bigg|_{m}=\bigg|\!\bigg|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})\left(s\left(\frac{X-1}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{a}\left(\frac{Y}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{b}\left(\frac{Z-1}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{c}\right)\bigg|\!\bigg|_{m^{\prime}}.

In particular,

\sup_{x\in L_{m}}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(x))|\!|_{m}=\sup_{x\in L_{m^{\prime}}}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(x))|\!|_{m^{\prime}}

for all
m^{\prime}\geq m
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows immediately from Lemma
4.3.2
. Indeed, calling

\alpha_{r}(s)=\sum_{I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}}\binom{k-r}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\ell}}^{-1}\binom{k}{r}^{-1}\binom{a}{r}f_{I}(\theta)(s),

which is independent of
m
or
m^{\prime}

we have, for all
m^{\prime}\geq m
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\!\bigg|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})\left(s\left(\frac{X-1}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{a}\left(\frac{Y}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{b}\left(\frac{Z-1}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{c}\right)\bigg|\!\bigg|_{m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\!\bigg|\sum_{r=0}^{\min(k,a)}\alpha_{r}(s)\left(\frac{X-1}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{a-r}\left(\frac{Y}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{b+r}\left(\frac{Z-1}{p^{m^{\prime}}}\right)^{c}\bigg|\!\bigg|_{m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{r}|\!|\alpha_{r}(s)|\!|,

which is independent of
m^{\prime}
. The last assertion follows from the first one. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.3.4
.

Assume that
\theta
extends to a continuous action on
S^{+}
. Then the operator
\nabla_{\theta}
extends uniquely to a locally analytic action on
V_{m}
for any
m\geq 0
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.3.3
and a change of coordinates, we can assume
m=0
. By Lemma
4.2.2
, it suffices to show that, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that for all
k\geq 0
we have

p^{-k\varepsilon}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})|\!|\leq C_{\varepsilon},

i.e. for all
F\in V_{0}
, we have

p^{-k\varepsilon}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla_{\theta})(F)|\!|\leq C_{\varepsilon}|\!|F|\!|.

It suffices to prove this for
F=sX^{a}Y^{b}Z^{c}
with
s\in S^{+}
. By Lemma
4.3.2
, it suffices to show that, for each
0\leq r\leq\min(k,a)
and any
I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}
, the value

p^{-k\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\binom{k-r}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\ell}}^{-1}\binom{k}{r}^{-1}\binom{a}{r}f_{I}(\theta)(s)\right|\!\right|

is uniformly bounded. But, since
|\!|f_{I}(\theta)|\!|\leq 1
(because
\theta
extends to a continuous action on
S^{+}
) this value is bounded above by
p^{-k\varepsilon+2\log_{p}(k)}
, which is uniformly bounded in
k
as
-k\varepsilon+2\log_{p}(k)\to-\infty
as
k\to+\infty
. This finishes the proof.
∎

4.4.
Overconvergence

We finish this section with the abstract results that will allow us to extend the locally analytic action from the ordinary locus to overconvergent neighbourhoods. The setting is as follows. Let

V_{0}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq V_{r}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq V_{\infty}

be a sequence of topological
R
-modules which are also
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-Banach spaces and let
\nabla:V_{\infty}\to V_{\infty}
be a continuous
R
-linear operator stabilising each
V_{i}
. Let
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{i}
denote the Banach norm on
V_{i}
and suppose that
|\!|x|\!|_{s}\leq|\!|x|\!|_{r}
for all
x\in V_{r}
and
r\leq s\leq\infty
.

Proposition 4.4.1
.

Assume that the following property holds: for any
0<\delta<1
and
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists
s=s(\delta)\geq r
such that, for all
c\in\mathbb{Q}
,
h\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in V_{r}
we have

(4.4.2)

|\!|x|\!|_{r}\leq p^{c}\text{ and }|\!|x|\!|_{\infty}\leq p^{c-h}\implies|\!|x|\!|_{s}\leq p^{c-\delta h}.

Assume that, for some
\varepsilon>0
, the operator
\nabla
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
V_{\infty}
. Then, for any
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma>\varepsilon
, there exists
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
(depending only on
\varepsilon
,
\gamma
and
|\!|\nabla|\!|_{r}
) such that, for any
x\in V_{r}
,

p^{-k\gamma}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla)(x)|\!|_{s}\to 0\text{ as }k\to+\infty.

In particular, the operator
\nabla
extends to a continuous
R
-linear action
C_{\gamma}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times V_{r}\to V_{s}
.

Proof.

Let
x\in V_{r}
be such that
|\!|x|\!|_{r}\leq 1
(and hence
|\!|x|\!|_{\infty}\leq 1
), and let
C_{r}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
be a constant such that
|\!|\nabla|\!|_{r}\leq p^{C_{r}}
. From the inequality
|\!|\nabla|\!|_{r}\leq p^{C_{r}}
we deduce that

|\!|k!f_{k}(\nabla)(x)|\!|_{r}\leq p^{kC_{r}}.

On the other hand, since
\nabla
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
V_{\infty}
, there exists
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

|\!|k!f_{k}(\nabla)(x)|\!|_{\infty}\leq p^{-v_{p}(k!)+C_{\varepsilon}+k\varepsilon}=p^{kC_{r}-(kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!))}

for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
k
be large enough such that
kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!)>0
, which is always possible as soon as
C_{r}\geq\varepsilon
. Applying (
4.4.2
) to the element
k!f_{k}(\nabla)(x)
with
c=kC_{r}
and
h=kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!)>0
(which we may assume to be an integer) we deduce that, for any
\delta<1
, there exists
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

|\!|k!f_{k}(\nabla)(x)|\!|_{s}\leq p^{kC_{r}-\delta(kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!))}.

Now let
\gamma>\varepsilon
. We obtain

(4.4.3)

\displaystyle p^{-k\gamma}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla)(x)|\!|_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{kC_{r}-\delta kC_{r}+\delta C_{\varepsilon}+k\delta\varepsilon-\delta v_{p}(k!)-k\gamma+v_{p}(k!)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{-k\left((\delta-1)C_{r}+(\gamma-\delta\varepsilon)+(\delta-1)\right)+\delta C_{\varepsilon}}

where the last inequality follows from
v_{p}(k!)\leq k
. One can easily show that one can choose
\delta
(that will depend on
\varepsilon
,
\gamma
and
C_{r}
) in such a way that
\left((\delta-1)C_{r}+(\gamma-\delta\varepsilon)+(\delta-1)\right)>0
which implies that (
4.4.3
) goes to
0
as
k\to+\infty
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.4.4
.

It is not necessary that the maps
V_{0}\to V_{1}\to\cdots\to V_{\infty}
are injective, and the above proof still holds without this assumption.

5.
p
-adic interpolation of the Gauss-Manin connection

This section is devoted to the proof of the assertion of Theorem
2.3.2
concerning the existence of the action of locally analytic functions on the space of nearly overconvergent modular forms. We will establish this by first proving a local version over some affinoid
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}
, and then explain how the construction globalises. We assume throughout that
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
is the adic generic fibre of an open formal subscheme
\operatorname{Spf}A^{+}\subset\mathfrak{X}
.

5.1.
The Gauss–Manin connection

Recall from Appendix
A
that
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}=\operatorname{VB}^{\operatorname{can}}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}})
is naturally a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module, where
\pi\colon P_{\operatorname{dR}}\to X
denotes the structural map and
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}
denotes sections of the lower-triangular Borel
\overline{P}\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. The
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{P})
-module
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}
carries some additional structure, namely
\overline{P}
acts through
algebra
automorphisms of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}
and
\mathfrak{g}
acts through
derivations

\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}
. Therefore we obtain a derivation
\nabla\colon\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}\to\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}
as the composition

(5.1.1)

\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}\to\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega^{1}_{X/R}(\operatorname{log}D)\to\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}

where the first map is induced from the
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module structure, and the second is induced from the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism
\Omega^{1}_{X/R}(\operatorname{log}D)\cong\omega_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}}
, the adjoint map to the universal trivialisation
\pi^{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}
, and the multiplication structure on
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}
.

Since
\pi
is affine, the derivation
\nabla
is equivalent to a derivation
\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}
. We also let
\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}
denote the corresponding derivation on the associated adic space. This immediately induces a derivation

\nabla\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}

where
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}=\operatorname{colim}_{U}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{U}
is the colimit over all quasi-compact strict open neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
in
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
(with structural map
\pi\colon U\to\mathcal{X}
). This is compatible with the Atkin–Serre operator via the map to
p
-adic modular forms.

5.1.1.
Local description

We note the following local description of
\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}
, which follows from the way the
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module structure is constructed in the proof of Lemma
A.1.3
. Let
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}
be a quasi-compact open affinoid subspace as above, over which
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
\omega_{\mathcal{E}}
,
\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}}
trivialise. Let
\{e,f\}
denote a basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}
respecting the Hodge filtration, and let
\bar{f}\in\omega_{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}
denote the image of
f
under the projection
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}\twoheadrightarrow\omega_{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}
(we also denote this projection by
\overline{(-)}
). Via the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism, there exists a unique differential
\kappa\in\Omega^{1}_{A/R}(\operatorname{log}D)
such that

\overline{\nabla(e)}=\bar{f}\otimes\kappa\in\omega_{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{A/R}(\operatorname{log}D).

Let
D\colon A\to A
denote the derivation dual to
\kappa
, then we can write

\nabla_{D}((f\;e))=(f\;e)\cdot\delta

for some
\delta\in\mathfrak{g}_{A}
. Let
F\in A[U,T_{1},T_{2},T_{1}^{-1},T_{2}^{-1}]\subset\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}(P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}})
be a polynomial, which we view as an element of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A=A[U,T_{1},T_{2},T_{1}^{-1},T_{2}^{-1}]
(here we write a general element in
\overline{P}
as
\left(\begin{matrix}T_{2}&\\
U&T_{1}\end{matrix}\right)
). Then the action of
\nabla
is described as

\nabla(F)(U,T_{1},T_{2})=\left(D\cdot F(U,T_{1},T_{2})+\delta\star_{l}F(U,T_{1},T_{2})\right)\cdot T_{1}T_{2}^{-1}

where
D\cdot F(U,T_{1},T_{2})
is the application of the derivation
D
on the coefficients, and
\star_{l}
denotes the
\mathfrak{g}
-action on
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}
. The extra factor
T_{1}T_{2}^{-1}
arises from the second map in (
5.1.1
).

Example 5.1.2
.

Suppose that
\delta=\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)
. Then the action
\delta\star_{l}-
is given by the differential
UT_{1}T_{2}^{-1}\partial_{T_{1}}-U\partial_{T_{2}}
.

5.2.
The local construction

Fix a quasi-compact open affinoid
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}
as in §
5.1.1
. Let
n\geq 1
be an integer. In this subsection, we will prove the local version of Theorem
2.3.2
using results from §
3
and §
4
. The first step is to understand the derivation

\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}

where
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
is as in Definition
3.4.1
. Note that, since
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}
is a pro-étale
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsor, this derivation extends uniquely to a derivation
\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n,A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n,A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}}}
, and via the decomposition in Lemma
3.3.2
and the local description in §
5.1.1
, this decomposes as

\nabla=\oplus_{\sigma_{\lambda}\in\mathscr{G}_{n}}\nabla^{\lambda}\colon\bigoplus_{\lambda\in T(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\sigma_{\lambda}(g_{\infty}),p^{-n})}\to\bigoplus_{\lambda\in T(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\sigma_{\lambda}(g_{\infty}),p^{-n})}.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer and
\sigma_{\lambda}\in\mathscr{G}_{\infty}
corresponding to
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. For ease of notation, set
\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}=\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\sigma_{\lambda}(g_{\infty}),p^{-n})
.

(1)

The operator
\nabla^{\lambda}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}}(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}}(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda})
is integral, i.e. it preserves
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}}(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda})
. Moreover, the operator
\nabla^{\lambda}
has the following explicit description:
via the
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
-algebra isomorphism

(5.2.2)

\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}}(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda})\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\frac{X-1}{p^{n}},\frac{Y}{p^{n}},\frac{Z-1}{p^{n}}\rangle

given by sending
T_{1}\mapsto\sigma_{\lambda}(\alpha_{\infty})X
,
U\mapsto\sigma_{\lambda}(\beta_{\infty})X+\sigma_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\infty})Y
,
T_{2}\mapsto\sigma_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\infty})ZX^{-1}
, the operator
\nabla^{\lambda}
is an
R^{+}
-linear derivation such that

•

\nabla^{\lambda}(g)\equiv s_{\lambda}\cdot\theta(g)
(mod
p^{n}
) for some
s_{\lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and all
g\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}

•

\nabla^{\lambda}(X)\equiv Y
(mod
p^{n}
), and
\nabla^{\lambda}(Y)=\nabla^{\lambda}(Z)=0

where
\theta\colon A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\to A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
is the Atkin–Serre differential operator.

(2)

The operator
\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n})
is integral, i.e. it preserves
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n})
.

Proof.

First recall that for
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
we have
\sigma_{\lambda}(\alpha_{\infty})=\lambda_{1}\alpha_{\infty}
,
\sigma_{\lambda}(\beta_{\infty})=\lambda_{1}\beta_{\infty}
and
\sigma_{\lambda}(\gamma_{\infty})=\lambda_{2}\gamma_{\infty}
. Via the local description in §
5.1.1
, we can calculate the Gauss–Manin connection using the basis
\{\lambda_{1}^{-1}e_{\infty},\lambda_{2}^{-1}f_{\infty}\}
of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}
over
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
. Let
\kappa\in\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}/R}(\operatorname{log}D)
denote the unique differential satisfying

\overline{\nabla(\lambda_{1}^{-1}e_{\infty})}=\lambda_{2}^{-1}\bar{f}_{\infty}\otimes\kappa

and let
D\colon A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\to A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
denote the derivation dual to
\kappa
. It is well-known (see
[
How20
]
for example) that
D=s_{\lambda}\theta
for some
s_{\lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
(this factor arises from comparing the universal trivialisations over
\mathfrak{IG}_{\infty}
via the polarisation on
\mathcal{E}
). Let
\delta\in\mathfrak{g}_{A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}}
be the element such that

\nabla_{D}(\lambda_{2}^{-1}f_{\infty}\;\lambda_{1}^{-1}e_{\infty})=(\lambda_{2}^{-1}f_{\infty}\;\lambda_{1}^{-1}e_{\infty})\cdot\delta

We have that
\delta=\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)
. The coordinates corresponding to this basis are given by
X,Y,Z^{\prime}
satisfying the identity

\left(\begin{matrix}T_{2}&\\
U&T_{1}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}Z^{\prime}&\\
Y&X\end{matrix}\right)\sigma_{\lambda}(g_{\infty}),\quad\quad g_{\infty}=\left(\begin{matrix}\gamma_{\infty}&\\
\beta_{\infty}&\alpha_{\infty}\end{matrix}\right)

and we find that the action of
\delta\star_{l}-
on
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
is given by
YX(Z^{\prime})^{-1}\partial_{X}-Y\partial_{Z^{\prime}}
(see Example
5.1.2
). Set
Z=Z^{\prime}X
. Then we calculate that

•

\nabla^{\lambda}(g)=(Dg)\cdot X(Z^{\prime})^{-1}=s_{\lambda}\cdot\theta(g)X^{2}Z^{-1}
for any
g\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}

•

\nabla^{\lambda}(X)=YX(Z^{\prime})^{-1}\cdot X(Z^{\prime})^{-1}=YX^{4}Z^{-2}

•

\nabla^{\lambda}(Y)=0

•

\nabla^{\lambda}(Z)=\nabla^{\lambda}(Z^{\prime}X)=(-YX(Z^{\prime})^{-1})X+Z^{\prime}YX^{4}Z^{-2}=-YX^{3}Z^{-1}+YX^{3}Z^{-1}=0
.

This completes the proof of the first part of the proposition since
X\equiv Z\equiv 1
(mod
p^{n}
). Part (2) follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{IG}_{\infty}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}
is a proetale
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsor, so it is enough to check integrality of
\nabla
after base-change along this torsor.
∎

We are now in a position to apply the general results in §
4
as we have an integral derivation
\nabla
which is congruent mod
p^{n}
to a nilpotent derivation that extends the Atkin–Serre operator. Recall that the Atkin–Serre operator
\theta\colon A^{+}_{\infty}\to A^{+}_{\infty}
extends to a
R^{+}
-algebra action of
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R^{+})
. Set
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n})
. We have the following proposition:

Proposition 5.2.3
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Then there exists
n(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any
n\geq n(\varepsilon)
there exists a unique continuous
R
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}\to\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}

extending
\nabla\colon\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}\to\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}
.

Proof.

It is enough to check this for the operators
\nabla^{\lambda}
introduced at the start of this section. Indeed, the operator
\nabla=\oplus_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}
is Galois invariant and the map
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}\hat{\otimes}_{A_{\operatorname{ord}}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
is an isometry (as
\mathfrak{IG}_{\infty}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}
is a proétale
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsor). In this case, we have that

•

By Proposition
5.2.1
, the derivation
\nabla^{\lambda}
is congruent modulo
p^{n}
to the operator “
\nabla_{s_{\lambda}\theta}
” defined in §
4.3
, extending the derivation
s_{\lambda}\theta
;

•

The derivation
s_{\lambda}\theta
extends to an action of
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R^{+})
.

Therefore the result follows from Propositions
4.2.4
and
4.3.4
. The action is unique because
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Banach space and we can compute the action using Mahler expansions.
∎

We now overconverge the above proposition. For any
n\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{U}_{n}
denote an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
as in Definition
3.4.1
, and set
\mathcal{U}_{n,r}=\mathcal{U}_{n}\cap P_{\operatorname{dR},A_{r}}^{\operatorname{an}}
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathcal{U}_{n}=\mathcal{U}_{n,1}
. Set
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{U}_{n,r}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{n,r}}(\mathcal{U}_{n,r})
, and recall from §
3.4
that we have a chain of restriction maps

(5.2.4)

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{U}_{n}}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{U}_{n,1}}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{U}_{n,2}}\to\cdots\to\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}}

induced from the inclusions
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT,n}}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{U}_{n,2}\subset\mathcal{U}_{n,1}=\mathcal{U}_{n}
. Recall that Corollary
3.4.4
holds for this chain of maps.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Then for any quasi-compact strict open neighbourhood
U
of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}
in
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
, there exists a quasi-compact strict open neighbourhood
V\subseteq U
of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}
in
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
and a unique continuous
R
-linear action

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{U}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{V}

extending the action of polynomial functions in
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
induced from the operator
\nabla\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{U}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{U}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{V}
. Moreover, these actions are all compatible if one changes
\varepsilon
,
U
or
V
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 1
be such that
n\geq n(\varepsilon/2)
as in Proposition
5.2.3
and such that
\mathcal{U}\subset U
for some overconvergent extension
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
(i.e., we can find a sufficiently large integer
n
such that
U
contains an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
; this is always possible since overconvergent extensions are cofinal by Corollary
3.3.3
). Then, by Proposition
4.4.1
, there exists some
r>0
such that
\nabla
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic function on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{U}_{n,r}}
. This implies the result with
V=\mathcal{U}_{n,r}
. The last part follows from the unicity of the action.
∎

5.3.
The global construction

We now explain how the construction in the previous subsection globalises. Let
U
be any quasi-compact open strict neighbourhood of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
in
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
with structural map
\pi\colon U\to\mathcal{X}
. Set
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{U}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{U}
. Let
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}
be a quasi-compact open affinoid as in the previous section. Let
U_{A}=U\times_{\mathcal{X}}\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
and note that
U_{A}
is a quasi-compact strict open neighbourhood of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty,A}
in
P_{\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
. We have
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{U}(\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+}))=\mathcal{O}_{U_{A}}(U_{A})
.

By Proposition
5.2.5
(and passing to the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
), there exists a continuous
R
-linear action

C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{U}(\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+}))\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}(\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+}))

which is compatible with changing
U
. Furthermore, by the unicity property in Proposition
5.2.5
, this action is functorial in
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
. Since the opens
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}
satisfying the conditions in the previous subsection are stable under finite intersections and cover
\mathcal{X}
, we obtain an induced action

C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)\times\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{N}^{\dagger})\to\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{N}^{\dagger})

extending the action of
\nabla
. This action is unique on global sections because
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}=\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{N}^{\dagger})=\operatorname{colim}_{U}\operatorname{H}^{0}(U,\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}})
as
U
runs over all quasi-compact open strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
(the topological space
\mathcal{X}
is quasi-compact and satisfies condition (4) in
[
Sta22
, Tag 009F]
), and one can choose a cofinal system of quasi-compact open neighbourhoods such that the transition maps in this colimit are injective (see Proposition
6.2.1
). One then applies Lemma
4.2.3
. This completes the proof of the locally analytic action in Theorem
2.3.2
.

6.
Additional structures on nearly overconvergent modular forms

In this section we study various natural structures on the space of nearly overconvergent modular forms. We start by showing that
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
is equipped with a natural action of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and define (nearly) overconvergent modular forms of weight
\kappa
as the
(-w_{0}\kappa)
-isotypic component for this action, where
w_{0}
denotes the longest Weyl element of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. We then define a filtration on
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
and the
U_{p}
-operator, and study how all of these operators interact with each other. We will finally conclude with the construction of the overconvergent projector, the relation with
p
-depletion, and a comparison with the spaces of nearly overconvergent modular forms introduced in
[
AI21
]
.

6.1.
The group action

The definition of the
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on (nearly) overconvergent modular forms is almost immediate from the construction. Indeed, by viewing
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
as a subgroup of
T^{\operatorname{an}}\subset P^{\operatorname{an}}
, we obtain an action of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
. Since
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
is stable under this action, we see that the
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
maps any (quasi-compact) strict open neighbourhood of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
into another (quasi-compact) strict open neighbourhood; hence we obtain an action of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
. By exactly the same argument for
M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
, we also obtain a
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
, and these actions are compatible via the natural maps
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{M}
. Here
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{colim}_{U}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{U}
where the colimit runs over all strict open neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
in
M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
(with structural map
\pi\colon U\to\mathcal{X}
).

Definition 6.1.1
.

Let
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
be a locally analytic character. We define the sheaf of nearly overconvergent modular forms of weight
\kappa
as

\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}:=\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}[-w_{0}\kappa]=\operatorname{Hom}_{T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(-w_{0}\kappa,\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}),

i.e. the
(-w_{0}\kappa)
-isotypic part for the action of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
. We define
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
analogously and we set
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
(resp.
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
) to be the global sections of
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
).

We note that our space of overconvergent modular forms of a specified weight agrees with the construction in
[
AIP18
]
. We begin with the following lemma:

Lemma 6.1.2
.

For every integer
r\geq 1
,
M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{r}
has a reduction of structure to an étale torsor
\mathcal{F}_{r}\to\mathcal{X}_{r}
for the group

\mathcal{T}_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\left(1+p^{r+1-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}\right)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\left(1+p^{r+1-\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}\right)\subset\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{an}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{an}}=T

where
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle t\rangle,\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle t\rangle)
denotes the unit ball. Furthermore, the torsors
\{\mathcal{F}_{r}\}_{r\geq 1}
form a cofinal system of strict quasi-compact open neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
in
M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from the results in
[
Pil13
]
and
[
AIS14
]
(see also
[
BP22
, Proposition 5.15]
).
∎

This leads to the following proposition.

Proposition 6.1.3
.

Let
\kappa:T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
be a locally analytic character. Then
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\dagger}
-module of rank one, where
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\dagger}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{colim}_{r}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}
. Moreover, it is equal to the sheaf of weight
\kappa
overconvergent modular forms as in
[
AIP18
]
.
4
4

4

Note that, in
[
AIP18
]
, the authors define overconvergent modular forms with weight given by a locally analytic character on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, which implicitly uses the polarisation on
\mathcal{E}
. However, the extension to characters of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
easily follows from their methods.

Proof.

As above,
\mathcal{F}_{r}\to\mathcal{X}_{r}
denotes the étale
\mathcal{T}_{r}
-torsor providing a reduction of structure of
M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{r}
. The proposition follows immediately from the fact that the torsors
\{\mathcal{F}_{r}\}_{r\geq 1}
form a cofinal system of quasi-compact strict open neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
in
M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
.
∎

We finish this subsection by describing how the locally analytic action in §
5
interacts with the group action.

Lemma 6.1.4
.

Let
\phi\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
. Then for any
t=\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},t_{2})\in T({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, we have

t\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \phi=\phi(t_{2}^{-1}\cdot-\cdot t_{1})\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ t

as endomorphisms of
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
is a (topological)
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
-module and polynomial functions are dense in
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
, it suffices to check this with
\phi
equal to the structural map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow R
, i.e. one has
t\circ\nabla=t_{2}^{-1}t_{1}\nabla\circ t
as endomorphisms of
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
. This amounts to checking the relation on
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}=\operatorname{VB}^{\operatorname{can}}_{K}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{P}})
. But this just follows from the description of the
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-module structure in Lemma
A.1.3
. Indeed, if we identify the
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-module associated with
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{P}}
with
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}
(where
\pi\colon G\to\operatorname{FL}=G/\overline{P}
is the structural map), then the action of
\nabla
corresponds to

f\mapsto\left(g\mapsto(\operatorname{Ad}(g)X\star_{l}f)(g)+(X\star_{r}f)(g)\right),\quad\quad f\in\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}

where
\star_{l}
(resp
\star_{r}
) denotes the
\mathfrak{g}
-action obtained from left-translation (resp. right-translation) of the argument and
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. The action of
t
is given by right-translation of the argument (i.e. the torsor structure), so the relation follows from
\operatorname{Ad}(t)X=t_{2}^{-1}t_{1}X
.
∎

6.2.
Reductions of structure of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}

It turns out that we can extend the torsors in Lemma
6.1.2
to reductions of structure of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
. More precisely, for any integer
r\geq 1
, let

\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cdot\left\{x\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}:x\equiv 1\text{ modulo }p^{r+1-\frac{1}{p-1}}\right\}

where
\overline{\mathcal{P}}
denotes the adic generic fibre of the formal
p
-adic completion of
\overline{P}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.

Proposition 6.2.1
.

For any integer
r\geq 1
, there exists an integer
s\geq r
and an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
-torsor
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
such that

(1)

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
provides a reduction of structure of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{s}
(to an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
-torsor) and
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
provides a reduction of structure (to a proétale
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsor) of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
. In particular
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
is a quasi-compact open subset of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
containing the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
.

(2)

The pushout
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\times^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}}\mathcal{T}_{r}
along the natural projection
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{r}
coincides with
\mathcal{F}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}\mathcal{X}_{s}
.

Moreover, for any two étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
-torsors
\mathscr{F}
and
\mathscr{G}
over
\mathcal{X}_{s}
satisfying (1), there exists an integer
s^{\prime}\geq s
such that we have an identification of torsors

(6.2.2)

\mathscr{F}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\mathcal{X}_{s^{\prime}}=\mathscr{G}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\mathcal{X}_{s^{\prime}}

(viewed as open subspaces of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
). The collection of torsors
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\}_{r,s}
as
r,s
vary form a cofinal system of quasi-compact open subsets of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
containing the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{IG}_{r,s}
denote the pushout of
\mathcal{F}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}\mathcal{X}_{s}
along the natural map
\mathcal{T}_{r}\twoheadrightarrow T(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r+\delta}\mathbb{Z})
, where
\delta=0
(resp.
\delta=1
) if
p=2
(resp.
p
is odd). We first establish existence of the torsors
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
. Note that we have a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{r,\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{r,s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{s}}

where
\mathcal{IG}_{r,\infty}\to\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
denotes the Igusa cover with Galois group
T(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r+\delta}\mathbb{Z})
. Let
\{\operatorname{Spf}A^{+,(i)}\}_{i\in I}
be a finite open cover of
\mathfrak{X}
over which
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{E}}
trivialise, and let
U^{(i)}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{(i)},A^{+,(i)})
denote the adic generic fibre of
\operatorname{Spf}A^{+,(i)}
. Let
U_{s}^{(i)}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{(i)}_{s},A^{+,(i)}_{s})
and
U_{r,s}^{(i)}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{(i)}_{r,s},A^{+,(i)}_{r,s})
denote the pullbacks of
U^{(i)}
to
\mathcal{X}_{s}
and
\mathcal{IG}_{r,s}
respectively. For
(i,j)\in I\times I
, set

U^{(i,j)}_{r,s}=U^{(i)}_{r,s}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}U^{(j)}_{r,s}=\operatorname{Spa}(B_{r,s}^{(i,j)},B_{r,s}^{+,(i,j)}).

Note that
A^{+,(i)}_{r,\infty}=A^{+,(i)}_{r,s}\langle 1/h\rangle
and
B_{r,\infty}^{+,(i,j)}=B_{r,s}^{+,(i,j)}\langle 1/h\rangle
, where
h
is a local lift of the Hasse invariant, and the natural restriction maps
A^{+,(i)}_{r,s}\to A^{+,(i)}_{r,\infty}
and
B_{r,s}^{+,(i,j)}\to B_{r,\infty}^{+,(i,j)}
are injective (since all of these open subspaces arise from a cover of the formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
). From the construction of
\mathcal{F}_{r}
(see
[
AIP18
]
), we observe that
\mathcal{F}_{r}
has sections over
U^{(i)}_{r,s}
(for any
s\geq r
).

Let
t_{i}\colon U^{(i)}\to P_{\operatorname{dR},A^{(i)}}^{\operatorname{an}}
be a section of the torsor
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to\mathcal{X}
. Then a section of
\mathcal{F}_{r+1}
over
U_{r+1,r+1}^{(i)}
can be described as

u_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}t_{i}\cdot g_{i}\colon U_{r+1,r+1}^{(i)}\to\mathcal{F}_{r+1}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r+1}}U_{r+1,r+1}^{(i)}

for some
g_{i}\in T^{\operatorname{an}}(A_{r+1,r+1}^{(i)})
. We can choose the sections
t_{i}
such that we have

t_{i}|_{U^{(i)}\cap U^{(j)}}=t_{j}|_{U^{(i)}\cap U^{(j)}}\cdot x_{i,j}

with
x_{i,j}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}(A^{(i,j)})
. Then the image of
g_{j}^{-1}x_{i,j}g_{i}
under the projection
\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}\to T^{\operatorname{an}}
is contained in
\mathcal{T}_{r+1}(B^{(i,j)}_{r+1,r+1})
. Let

\widetilde{u}_{i}\colon U_{r+1,\infty}^{(i)}\xrightarrow{u_{i}}\mathcal{F}_{r+1}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r+1}}U_{r+1,\infty}^{(i)}\to P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}U_{r+1,\infty}^{(i)}

where the second map is given by the unit root splitting. Then we can write
\widetilde{u}_{i}=t_{i}\cdot\widetilde{g}_{i}
for some
\widetilde{g}_{i}\in\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}(A^{(i)}_{r+1,\infty})
whose image under the projection
\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}(A^{(i)}_{r+1,\infty})\to T^{\operatorname{an}}(A^{(i)}_{r+1,\infty})
coincides with
g_{i}
. Furthermore, we have
\widetilde{g}_{j}^{-1}x_{i,j}\widetilde{g}_{i}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r+1}(B_{r+1,\infty}^{(i,j)})
.

By using the fact that
A^{+,(i)}_{r+1,\infty}=A^{+,(i)}_{r+1,r+1}\langle 1/h\rangle
, we can find elements
h_{i}\in\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}(A_{r+1,r+1}^{(i)})
such that
\widetilde{g}_{i}^{-1}h_{i}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r+1}(A_{r+1,\infty}^{(i)})
. Set
v_{i,j}=h_{j}^{-1}x_{i,j}h_{i}\in\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}(B_{r+1,r+1}^{(i,j)})
. Then we see that (because
I
is finite) we can find a sufficiently large integer
s\gg r+1
such that
v_{i,j}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(B_{r+1,s}^{(i,j)})
. We may also choose
s
such that the image of
h_{i}
under the projection map
\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}\to T^{\operatorname{an}}
(denoted
h_{i}^{\prime}
) satisfies
g_{i}^{-1}h^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{r}(A_{r+1,s}^{(i)})
. The étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
-torsor
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
is then defined by the collection of sections

\{t_{i}\cdot h_{i}\colon U_{r+1,s}^{(i)}\to P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}:i\in I\},

or alternatively, by the transition matrices
\{v_{i,j}:(i,j)\in I\times I\}
. The cocycle condition is automatic, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
provides a reduction of structure of
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
by construction. Clearly the pushout of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
along the map
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}\to\mathcal{T}_{r}
is given by the sections
\{t_{i}\cdot h_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
, which describes the torsor
\mathcal{F}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}\mathcal{X}_{s}
(by the condition
g_{i}^{-1}h^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{r}(A_{r+1,s}^{(i)})
and the fact that
\mathcal{F}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}\mathcal{X}_{s}
is the pushout of
\mathcal{F}_{r+1}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r+1}}\mathcal{X}_{s}
along the map
\mathcal{T}_{r+1}\to\mathcal{T}_{r}
). Finally, the pullback
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
is described by the orbit

\left(\mathcal{F}_{r+1}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r+1}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}\right)\cdot\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}\subset P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}

where we view
\left(\mathcal{F}_{r+1}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r+1}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}\right)\subset P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
via the unit root splitting. Therefore, we immediately see that
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
defines a reduction of structure of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
. This completes the proof of existence.

Suppose that
\mathscr{G}
is an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
-torsor over
\mathcal{X}_{s}
satisfying (1). Then it suffices to establish an identification of the form (
6.2.2
) for
\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s^{\prime}}
(with
s^{\prime}\geq s
). We will continue to use the same notation as above for the construction of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s^{\prime}}
. We have sections

\{t_{i}\cdot h_{i}\colon U_{r+1,s^{\prime}}^{(i)}\to P_{\operatorname{dR},A_{r+1,s^{\prime}}^{(i)}}^{\operatorname{an}}:i\in I\}

whose pullback to
U_{r+1,\infty}^{(i)}
give sections of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\times^{T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r+1}
. Since
\mathscr{G}
satisfies
(1)
, we see that
\mathscr{G}
is a strict neighbourhood of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\times^{T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r+1}
inside
P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
. This implies that there exists a sufficiently large integer
s^{\prime\prime}\gg s^{\prime}
such that
t_{i}\cdot h_{i}
factors as

U_{r+1,s^{\prime\prime}}^{(i)}\to\mathscr{G}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}U_{r+1,s^{\prime\prime}}^{(i)}\to P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}U_{r+1,s^{\prime\prime}}^{(i)}

i.e. we obtain a section of
\mathscr{G}
. This implies that
\mathscr{G}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\mathcal{X}_{s^{\prime\prime}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{s^{\prime}}}\mathcal{X}_{s^{\prime\prime}}
as required. Furthermore, one easily verifies that the system
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\}_{r,s}
is a cofinal collection of strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
.
∎

We consider the following representations and sheaves. Let
T^{+}\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the submonoid of diagonal matrices
\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},t_{2})
satisfying
v_{p}(t_{1})\geq v_{p}(t_{2})
. One has an isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
given by sending
(n_{1},n_{2})\times t
to the matrix
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n_{1}}&\\
&p^{n_{2}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)t
. We let
\langle\cdot\rangle\colon T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the projection to the second component.

Definition 6.2.3
.

Let
r\geq 1
and let
\Sigma_{r}=\mathcal{\overline{P}}_{r}\cdot T^{+}\cdot\mathcal{\overline{P}}_{r}
.

(1)

Let
V_{r}=\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r})
denote the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Banach algebra of global sections
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\operatorname{an}}
. We view this as a representation of
\Sigma_{r}
via the following action:

(p\star_{l}f)(-)=f(p^{-1}\cdot-),\quad(t\star_{l}f)(-)=f(t^{-1}\cdot-\cdot t\langle t\rangle^{-1}),\quad\quad p\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},t\in T^{+},f\in V_{r}.

(2)

If
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
is an
r
-analytic character, then we set

V_{r,\kappa}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{f\in V_{r}\hat{\otimes}R:f(xt)=(w_{0}\kappa)(t^{-1})f(x)\text{ for all }t\in\mathcal{T}_{r}\}.

This sub-Banach space is stable under the action of
\Sigma_{r}
.

(3)

For any torsor
\pi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
as in Proposition
6.2.1
, we let

\mathcal{V}_{r,s}=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}}\hat{\otimes}V_{r}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},\star_{l}}\cong\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}}

where the invariants are with respect to the
\star_{l}
-action above. This is a locally projective Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}
-modules in the sense of
[
BP21
, Definition 2.5.2]
, and comes equipped with an action
\star_{r}
of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
via the torsor structure (or equivalently via the action
(p\star_{r}f)(-)=f(-\cdot p)
on
V_{r}
). Similarly, we set

\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}}\hat{\otimes}V_{r,\kappa}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},\star_{l}}

which is also a locally projective Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}
-modules. We can view
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\subset\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
as the subsheaf of sections
f\in\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
satisfying
t\star_{r}f=(w_{0}\kappa)(t^{-1})f
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}_{r}
(recall that we are viewing
\mathcal{X}
as an adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
).

Remark 6.2.4
.

Note that
\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
and
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
depend on the choice of the torsor
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
, but for any two choices, one can always find an integer
s^{\prime}\geq s
such that the pullback of the sheaves to
\mathcal{X}_{s^{\prime}}
coincide (see Proposition
6.2.1
). We will therefore omit the choice of torsor from the notation.

Remark 6.2.5
.

Let
T^{++}\subset T^{+}
be the semi-group of diagonal matrices
\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},t_{2})
which satisfy
v_{p}(t_{1})>v_{p}(t_{2})
. Then, for any
t\in T^{++}
, we have
t\star_{l}V_{r+1,\kappa}\subset V_{r,\kappa}
. Since the inclusion
V_{r,\kappa}\to V_{r+1,\kappa}
is compact, we see that the operator
t\star_{l}-\colon V_{r+1,\kappa}\to V_{r+1,\kappa}
is also compact.

Since the system of torsors
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\}_{r,s}
is cofinal, we see that

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathscr{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)

where the transition maps are with respect to restriction and the natural inclusions
V_{r}\subset V_{r+1}
and
V_{r,\kappa}\subset V_{r+1,\kappa}
. We also have versions of this on the level of sheaves, namely

\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}.

Here we are implicitly viewing
\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
and
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
as sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules by pushing forward along the inclusion
\mathcal{X}_{s}\subset\mathcal{X}
. These descriptions are beneficial for the discussion of filtrations and Hecke operators below.

6.3.
Filtrations

We now define an ascending filtration on nearly overconvergent forms.

Definition 6.3.1
.

Let
r\geq 1
and
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
an
r
-analytic character.

(1)

For an integer
h\geq 0
, let
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}V_{r}\subset V_{r}
denote the sub-
\Sigma_{r}
-representation consisting of global sections
f\in V_{r}

\left(\begin{matrix}x&\\
y&z\end{matrix}\right)\mapsto f(x,y,z),\quad\quad\left(\begin{matrix}x&\\
y&z\end{matrix}\right)\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}

which are polynomial in the variable
y
of degree
\leq h
. This coincides with the subspace of elements in
V_{r}
killed by the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{h+1}
under
\star_{r}
. Similarly, we let
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}V_{r,\kappa}\subset V_{r,\kappa}
denote the subrepresentation of elements which are polynomial in the variable
y
of degree
\leq h
(or equivalently the elements killed by
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{h+1}
).

(2)

For an integer
h\geq 0
, let
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}\subset\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
and
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\subset\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
denote the subsheaves given by

\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}}\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Fil}_{h}V_{r}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},\star_{l}},\quad\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}}\hat{\otimes}\operatorname{Fil}_{h}V_{r,\kappa}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},\star_{l}}.

As in (1), these are the subsheaves killed by the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{h+1}
. The sheaf
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}
-module of finite rank which is independent of
r
. We set

\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}

where, as above, we are implicitly considering
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
and
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
as sheaves on
\mathcal{X}
by pushing forward along the inclusion
\mathcal{X}_{s}\subset\mathcal{X}
.

(3)

We let
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}=\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X},\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\right)
and
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}=\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X},\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}\right)
. Equivalently, we have

\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathscr{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right).

Remark 6.3.2
.

One can easily verify that
\operatorname{Fil}_{h}V_{r,\kappa}=\operatorname{Sym}^{h}\operatorname{St}\otimes R(w_{0}\kappa+h(1;-1))
, where
\operatorname{St}
is the standard representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
and
R(w_{0}\kappa+h(1;-1))
denotes the one-dimensional line given by the character
w_{0}\kappa+h(1;-1)
(inflated to
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
). If we view
\kappa
as a pair
(\kappa^{\prime};w)
satisfying
\kappa(t,st^{-1})=\kappa^{\prime}(t)w(s)
, then we have

\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}=\operatorname{Sym}^{h}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\left(\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}}^{\kappa^{\prime}-h+w}\otimes\omega_{\mathcal{E}}^{-w}\right)

which agrees with the definition given in
[
Urb14
]
(after identifying
\omega_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}}
via the polarisation).

Similarly, we have

\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}=\operatorname{Sym}^{h}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}\;\hat{\otimes}\;\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}}^{-h}\;\hat{\otimes}\;\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}\mathcal{X}_{s}}

where
\pi^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{F}_{r}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{r}}\mathcal{X}_{s}\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
denotes the structural map. One easily sees that
\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
and
\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathcal{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
.

6.3.1.
Compatibility with the Gauss–Manin connection

Let
U=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
be an étale morphism over which the torsor
\pi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
trivialises. Then any section
f\in\mathcal{V}_{r,s}(U)
is described as a morphism
f\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to V_{r}\hat{\otimes}B
such that
f(xp)=p^{-1}\star_{l}f(x)
for all
x\in\pi^{-1}(U)
and
p\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
. If we identify
\pi^{-1}(U)=U\times\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
then the connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{V}_{r,s}(U)
has the following description

\nabla(f)(p)=(D\cdot f(p)+\delta\star_{l}f(p))\cdot y

for
p\in U\times\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
and
f\in\mathcal{V}_{r,s}(U)
, where
D\colon B\to B
is some derivation and
\delta\in\mathfrak{g}_{B}
. Here
y\in\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,2\rho}(U)
is the element arising from the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism. If
f\in\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}(U)
then the action
\delta\star_{l}-
can increase the degree of
f(p)
in the unipotent variable by at most
1
, so we immediately see that:

Lemma 6.3.3
.

For all
h\geq 0
, one has
\nabla\left(\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}\right)\subset\operatorname{Fil}_{h+1}\mathcal{V}_{r,s}
.

Let
\operatorname{Gr}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}=\operatorname{Fil}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}/\operatorname{Fil}_{h-1}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
(with the convention
\operatorname{Fil}_{-1}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}=0
). We have the following lemma describing the connection on graded pieces.

Lemma 6.3.4
.

Let
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
be an
r
-analytic character which we view as a pair
(\kappa^{\prime};w)
of
r
-analytic characters
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to R^{\times}
such that
\kappa(t,st^{-1})=\kappa^{\prime}(t)w(s)
. Let
u_{\kappa}\in R
be the element such that
\kappa^{\prime}(t)=\operatorname{exp}(u_{\kappa}\operatorname{log}(t))
for
t\in 1+p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then

\nabla\colon\operatorname{Gr}_{h}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{h+1}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa+2\rho}

is an isomorphism multiplied by
u_{\kappa}-h
.

Proof.

By using the polarisation
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}^{D}
, we can identify this map with that in
[
AI21
, Theorem 3.18]
, from which the result follows.
∎

6.4.
The
U_{p}
-operator

Let
r\geq 1
. We have a correspondence

\mathcal{X}_{r}\xleftarrow{p_{1}}\mathcal{C}_{r}\xrightarrow{p_{2}}\mathcal{X}_{r+1}

where
\mathcal{C}_{r}
denotes the moduli of order
p
subgroups
C\subset\mathcal{E}[p]
which are disjoint from the canonical subgroup
H_{1}^{\operatorname{can}}
. The map
p_{1}
is the natural one, and
p_{2}(E)=E/C
is the quotient by the order
p
subgroup. Note that
p_{1}
is finite flat of degree
p
and
p_{2}
is an isomorphism. Let
(\mathcal{E},C)
denote the universal generalised elliptic curve and order
p
subgroup over
\mathcal{C}_{r}
, and set
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}/C
. Let
\lambda\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
denote the corresponding isogeny, and
\lambda^{D}\colon(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})^{D}\to\mathcal{E}^{D}
its dual. We have a natural isomorphism
\lambda_{*}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Definition 6.4.1
.

Let
P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}=P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{r}
. Let
\phi\colon p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}\to p_{2}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}
denote the isomorphism given by sending a trivialisation
\psi\colon\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{H}_{E}
to the trivialisation

\lambda_{*}\circ\psi\circ\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\colon\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{H}_{E/C}

where
C\subset E[p]
denotes the choice of order
p
subgroup which is disjoint from the canonical subgroup.

We consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{2}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{r+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

and we set
q_{2}=p_{2}\circ\phi
.

We have an analogue of this for Igusa towers over the ordinary locus, namely

\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\xleftarrow{p_{1}}p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\phi}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{p_{2}}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}

where the map
\phi
sends a pair of trivialisations
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
of
E[p^{\infty}]^{\circ}
and
E[p^{\infty}]^{\operatorname{et}}
respectively to
(\lambda\circ\psi_{1},p^{-1}\lambda\circ\psi_{2})
. This is well-defined because the canonical subgroup of
E
is
E[p]^{\circ}
, and
C
is étale. As above, we set
q_{2}=p_{2}\circ\phi
. Then we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}

with the left-hand square Cartesian.

If we let
\mathcal{Z}
(resp.
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
) denote the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
(resp.
p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
) inside
P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}
(resp.
p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}
) then we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}

Lemma 6.4.2
.

The left-hand side square above is Cartesian (i.e.
p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
).

Proof.

Clearly
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\subset p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})
. Now
p_{1}
is finite flat hence it is an open morphism. Suppose that
x\in p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})-\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
. Set
U=p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}-\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
which is open. Then
p_{1}(U)
is open and
p_{1}(x)\in p_{1}(U)\cap\mathcal{Z}
. Since
p_{1}(U)
is open, this means we must have
p_{1}(U)\cap\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\neq\varnothing
. But this implies that

U\cap p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{IG}_{\infty})=U\cap p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\neq\varnothing

which is a contradiction.
∎

We now have the following construction of the
U_{p}
-operator. Thanks to the above lemma, for any quasi-compact open
\mathcal{Z}\subset U\subset P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}
, there exists a quasi-compact open
\mathcal{Z}\subset U^{\prime}\subset P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}
such that
q_{2}^{-1}(U)\supset p_{1}^{-1}(U^{\prime})
. Therefore, we get maps
