\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}}(U)\xrightarrow{q_{2}^{*}}\mathcal{O}_{p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}(q_{2}^{-1}(U))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}\mathcal{O}_{p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}(p_{1}^{-1}(U^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}(U^{\prime})

where
\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}
denotes the trace map associated with
p_{1}
. All of these maps are compatible, so we obtain an induced map on the limit, namely the
U_{p}
-operator
U_{p}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
.

Remark 6.4.3
.

Exactly the same construction works for
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}
and the
U_{p}
-operators are compatible under the natural map
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
.

Remark 6.4.4
.

One also has similar constructions for the Frobenius operator
\varphi
and diamond operator
S_{p}
, which are defined with respect to the action of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
respectively. One can easily show that
U_{p}\circ\varphi=pS_{p}
and
S_{p}
commutes with
U_{p}
and
\varphi
. We leave the details to the reader.

6.4.1.
Compatibility with the group action

Recall that we have a cofinal system
\{U_{i}\}
of neighbourhoods of the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\to P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}
satisfying
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cdot U_{i}\subset U_{i}
. Here we are considering the action
T^{\operatorname{an}}\times P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}\to P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}
through the inclusion
T^{\operatorname{an}}\subset\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Lemma 6.4.5
.

The
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action commutes with
U_{p}
,
\varphi
and
S_{p}
on
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}
and
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

For
U_{p}
, we just need to show that the group action commutes with
p_{1}
and
q_{2}
. But this is clear (since
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
commutes with
\operatorname{diag}(1,p^{-1})
). The proofs of the claims for
\varphi
and
S_{p}
are identical.
∎

6.4.2.
Compatibility with the connection

Let
r\geq 1
be an integer. By the construction above, one has a map
R
-modules

U_{p}\colon\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r+1}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right).

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4.6
.

For any
\phi\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
, one has

•

\phi(p\cdot-)\circ U_{p}=U_{p}\circ\phi

•

\phi\circ\varphi=\varphi\circ\phi(p\cdot-)

•

\phi\circ S_{p}=S_{p}\circ\phi

as endomorphisms of
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

By the density of polynomial functions in
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
, it suffices to check this for
\phi
equal to the structural map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow R
(i.e. for the operator
\nabla
). The functoriality results in
[
AI21
, §6.2]
imply that
p\nabla\circ U_{p}=U_{p}\circ\nabla
on
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right)
. Furthermore
U_{p}\circ\nabla
and
p\nabla\circ U_{p}
are two continuous
R
-linear morphisms
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
extending
U_{p}\circ\nabla=p\nabla\circ U_{p}
on
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right)
for any
r\geq 1
. But any such extension must be unique (because
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},r}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right)
is dense in
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{U}
for any quasi-compact strict open neighbourhood
U
of the Igusa tower). This proves the first bullet point. The second and third bullet points are similar (again following from the functoriality results in
[
AI21
, §6.2]
and a density argument).
∎

6.4.3.
A reinterpretation

We can also reinterpret the construction of
U_{p}
using the torsors constructed in Proposition
6.2.1
. We begin with the following lemma:

Lemma 6.4.7
.

Let
r\geq 1
be an integer. Then there exists an integer
s\geq r
such that

p_{2}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r+1,s+1}\subset\phi\left(p_{1}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\right)\subset p_{2}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s+1}

where
\phi\colon p_{1}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},s}^{\operatorname{an}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{2}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},s+1}^{\operatorname{an}}
is the morphism in Definition
6.4.1
.

Proof.

Let
s\geq r
be an integer such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r+1,s+1}
exist, and recall that the map
p_{2}
is an isomorphism. Let
\mathscr{G}=\phi(p_{1}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s})
, which is an étale torsor under the group

t^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}t\subset\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},\quad\quad t=\left(\begin{matrix}1&\\
&p^{-1}\end{matrix}\right).

Then we see that the pushout
\mathscr{G}\times^{t^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}t}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
is an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
-torsor which is a reduction of structure of
p_{2}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{dR},s+1}^{\operatorname{an}}
. Furthermore,
p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
defines a reduction of structure of
\mathscr{G}
. Since
p_{2}
is an isomorphism, we see from Proposition
6.2.1
that we can increase
s
(if necessary) so that
\mathscr{G}\times^{t^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}t}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
coincides with
p_{2}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s+1}
, and
p_{2}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r+1,s+1}
is a reduction of structure of
\mathscr{G}
via the embedding
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r+1}\subset t^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}t
.
∎

Let
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to(R^{+})^{\times}
be an
r
-analytic character, and let
s\geq r
be a sufficiently large integer such that the conclusion of Lemma
6.4.7
holds. Let

\pi_{1}\colon p_{1}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\to\mathcal{C}_{s},\quad\quad\pi_{2}\colon p_{2}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r+1,s+1}\to\mathcal{C}_{s},\quad\quad\sigma\colon\mathscr{G}\to\mathcal{C}_{s}

denote the structural maps of the torsors, where
\mathscr{G}
is as in Lemma
6.4.7
. Let
t=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in T^{++}
. Then the morphism
\phi^{*}\otimes(t\star_{l}-)
induces a cohomological correspondence

\phi_{\kappa}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r,s+1,\kappa}=\left((\sigma)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{G}}\hat{\otimes}V_{r,\kappa}\right)^{t^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}t,\star_{l}}\to\left((\pi_{1})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{p_{1}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}}\hat{\otimes}V_{r,\kappa}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r},\star_{l}}=p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}

where the first equality holds because
V_{r,\kappa}
is a representation of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}
and
\mathscr{G}\times^{t^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}t}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}=p_{2}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s+1}
. The
U_{p}
-operator can then be seen as the following composition:

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s+1},\mathcal{V}_{r,s+1,\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{2}^{*}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{C}_{s},p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r,s+1,\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{\kappa}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{C}_{s},p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)

where the first map is induced from restriction. These operators are compatible as one varies
r,s
, and the resulting operator on the limit
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}=\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)
is precisely the
U_{p}
-operator constructed in the previous sections. For
s\geq r+1
sufficiently large, we have a factorisation:

(6.4.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s+1},\mathcal{V}_{r+1,s+1,\kappa}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s+1},\mathcal{V}_{r+1,s+1,\kappa}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{p}}

because the cohomological correspondence
p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r+1,s+1,\kappa}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\kappa}}p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r+1,s,\kappa}
factors through
p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\subset p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{r+1,s,\kappa}
(see Remark
6.2.5
).

Remark 6.4.9
.

Using this reinterpretation, one can easily see that
U_{p}
preserves the filtration
\operatorname{Fil}_{\bullet}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
.

6.4.4.
Slope decompositions

We now discuss the spectral theory of the
U_{p}
-operator on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
. Let
r\geq 1
be an integer. Let
R_{0}^{+}
be an admissible
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra,
R_{0}=R_{0}^{+}[1/p]
and consider the affinoid adic space
\mathcal{W}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Spa}(R_{0},R_{0}^{+})
. Suppose that we have an
r
-analytic analytic character
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R_{0}^{\times}
. We have the following:

Theorem 6.4.10
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{W}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
h\in\mathbb{Q}
. Then there exists an open affinoid neighbourhood
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\subset\mathcal{W}
of
x
such that
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
and
\operatorname{Fil}_{\bullet}\mathscr{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger}
have slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to
U_{p}
. Furthermore, there exists an integer
k
such that

(\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa})^{\leq h}=(\operatorname{Fil}_{k}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa})^{\leq h}.

Proof.

Since the inclusion
V_{r,\kappa}\to V_{r+1,\kappa}
is compact and
\mathcal{X}_{s+1}\Subset\mathcal{X}_{s}
is a strict inclusion, the factorisation in (
6.4.8
) (and the standard theory of slope decompositions) implies that
\mathscr{L}_{\kappa}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}\right)
admits a slope
\leq h
decomposition over a neighbourhood
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
of
x
in
\mathcal{W}
. Furthermore, one can check that as
k\to+\infty
, the norm of the operator

(t\star_{l}-)\colon\operatorname{Gr}_{k}V_{r,\kappa}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{k}V_{r,\kappa}

tends to zero. Hence there exists an integer
k\geq 0
such that
\mathscr{L}_{\kappa}^{\leq h}=(\operatorname{Fil}_{k}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa})^{\leq h}
. To conclude the proof, we note that the factorisation (
6.4.8
) implies that
U_{p}
is pointwise nilpotent on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}/\mathscr{L}_{\kappa}
, so
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
admits a slope
\leq h
decomposition and
(\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa})^{\leq h}=\mathscr{L}_{\kappa}^{\leq h}
.
∎

6.5.
Overconvergent projectors

We now have all the ingredients to define an overconvergent projector on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
. As in the previous subsection, let
R_{0}^{+}
be an admissible
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra,
R_{0}=R_{0}^{+}[1/p]
and set
\mathcal{W}=\operatorname{Spa}(R_{0},R_{0}^{+})
. Suppose that we have a locally analytic character
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R_{0}^{\times}
.

Theorem 6.5.1
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{W}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
h\in\mathbb{Q}
. Then there exists an open affinoid neighbourhood
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\subset\mathcal{W}
containing
x
and an overconvergent projector

\Pi^{\leq h,\operatorname{oc}}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}\to\left(\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}\right)^{\leq h}\otimes_{R}\operatorname{Frac}(R)

with finitely-many poles, interpolating the overconvergent projectors in
[
Urb14
, §3.3.4]
for classical specialisations.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.4.10
, it is enough to construct an overconvergent projector for
\left(\operatorname{Fil}_{k}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}\right)^{\leq h}
, and this follows from exactly the same arguments as in
[
AI21
, §3.9]
(using Lemma
6.3.4
).
∎

6.6.
p
-depletion

In this section we discuss the relation between the action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
and
p
-depletion. We begin with the following lemma.

Lemma 6.6.1
.

The action of
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
coincides with the operator
1-p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}
on
p
-adic modular forms
\mathscr{M}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
R=\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
. The space
\mathscr{M}
can be described as sections of
\mathcal{IG}_{K^{p}}
invariant under right-translation by
U_{P^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{int}}
. Then, by
p
-adic Fourier theory (see
[
How20
, §7.1]
), the action of
1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is given by

f\mapsto\frac{1}{p}\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p}}\left(\begin{matrix}1&\widetilde{\zeta}\\
&1\end{matrix}\right)\cdot f

for
f\in\mathscr{M}
, where
\widetilde{\zeta}\in\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}
denotes any lift of
\zeta
. Here
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\widetilde{\zeta}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is an element of
J_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
acting on a function by right-translation of the argument. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle(p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}U_{p})(f)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{\omega\in T_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/pT_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})}\left(\begin{matrix}p&\omega\\
&1\end{matrix}\right)\cdot f

\displaystyle\varphi(f)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{matrix}\right)\cdot f.

Hence
p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}
coincides with the action of
1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
and the result follows.
∎

We now prove an overconvergent version of this lemma.

Proposition 6.6.2
.

The action of
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
coincides with the operator
1-p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}
on nearly overconvergent modular forms
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

We first claim that the action of
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
preserves the filtration
\operatorname{Fil}_{\bullet}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
. This can be checked locally on sections of overconvergent extensions of some
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n}
, for
n
sufficiently large (see §
3.3
–
3.4
). If
\mathcal{U}
is such an overconvergent extension, then we have

\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n,A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},n,A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}}})=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in T(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\sigma_{\lambda}(g_{\infty}),p^{-n}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\sigma_{\lambda}(g_{\infty}),p^{-n})}\right)

respecting filtrations (it is equivariant for the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}
), so it suffices to check the claim for each direct summand in the right-hand side. But the action of
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
on such a direct summand is given by
\lim_{m\to+\infty}(\nabla^{\lambda})^{p^{m-1}(p-1)}
, and one can see from the explicit description of
\nabla^{\lambda}
in the proof of Proposition
5.2.1
that this operator preserves the filtration (it acts as
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
on the
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
-coefficients of the power series in the coordinates
X,Y,Z
).

Since
\bigcup_{r\geq 0}\operatorname{Fil}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
is dense in
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
it therefore suffices to show that the operator
T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}1-p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}-1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}=1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}-p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}
is zero on
\operatorname{Fil}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
, for any integer
r\geq 0
. But for any integer
r\geq 0
, we have a morphism

\operatorname{Fil}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Gr}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{M}

which is equivariant for the action of
T
. Hence Lemma
6.6.1
implies that
T
is zero on the graded pieces of
\operatorname{Fil}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
– in particular, we must have
T^{r+1}=0
on
\operatorname{Fil}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
. Finally, we note that
T
is idempotent. Indeed

T^{2}=1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}-p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}-p^{-1}1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}+p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}=1_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}-p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}=T

where we have used the fact that
p^{-1}S_{p}^{-1}\varphi U_{p}
is idempotent (because
U_{p}\varphi=pS_{p}
) and the relations in Lemma
6.4.6
. This proves that
T=T^{r+1}
is zero on
\operatorname{Fil}_{r}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
as required.
∎

6.7.
Comparison with the work of Andreatta–Iovita

We end this section by comparing the space of nearly overconvergent forms constructed in this article with the space constructed in
[
AI21
]
. For this, consider the following group

\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cdot\{x\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}:m(x)\equiv 1\text{ modulo }p^{r+1-\frac{1}{p-1}}\}

where
m\colon\overline{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{T}
denotes the projection to the torus. With notation as in Proposition
6.2.1
, consider the pushout
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}^{\operatorname{AI}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}\times^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}
. Set
V_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}=\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}})
which carries an action of
\Sigma_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}\cdot T^{+}\cdot\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}
by the exact same formulae as in Definition
6.2.3
. If
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
is an
r
-analytic character, then we set

V_{r,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Hom}_{T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\left(-w_{0}\kappa,V_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}\hat{\otimes}R\right).

We let
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}^{\operatorname{AI}}}\hat{\otimes}V_{r,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}}
denote the corresponding locally projective Banach sheaf on
\mathcal{X}_{s}
, where
\pi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}^{\operatorname{AI}}\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
denotes the structural map. Note that
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}
is independent of the choice of
r
such that
\kappa
is
r
-analytic (whereas the sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}
from Definition
6.2.3
very much depends on
r
).

Set
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}_{\kappa}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}\right)
, where the colimit is over the restriction maps. This space carries actions of
U_{p}
,
S_{p}
and
\varphi
by exactly the process as in §
6.4
, and
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}_{\kappa}
admits slope decompositions with respect to
U_{p}
(up to possibly shrinking
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
). We have the following comparison result.

Proposition 6.7.1
.

Let
I=[0,p^{b}]
with
b\neq\infty
, and suppose that
k
is the universal character of the open
\mathcal{W}_{I}
of weight space introduced in
[
AI21
, p.2004]
. Let
\kappa=(k;w)\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{W}_{I})^{\times}
denote any locally analytic character extending
k
. Then:

(1)

For
s
sufficiently large, the sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}
is identified with the sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k,I}[1/p]
of nearly overconvergent forms over
\mathcal{X}_{s}
in
[
AI21
, §3.3]
.

(2)

The actions of
p^{-1}U_{p}
and
S_{p}^{-1}\varphi
on

(6.7.2)

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}_{\kappa}=\varinjlim_{s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathbb{W}_{k,I}[1/p]\right)

coincide with the actions of
U
and
V
constructed in
[
AI21
, §3.6–§3.7]
.

(3)

The Gauss–Manin connection
\nabla\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}_{\kappa}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}_{\kappa+2\rho}
intertwines with the connection constructed in
[
AI21
, §3.4]
under the identifications in (
6.7.2
).

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#},s_{\operatorname{can}})\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
denote the adic generic fibre of the formal vector bundle with marked section as constructed in
[
AI21
, §3.3]
. Here, we use the notation
\Omega_{E}\subset\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#}
(resp.
s_{\operatorname{can}}\in\Omega_{E}/p^{r+1-1/(p-1)}
) in place of the notation
\Omega_{E}\subset\operatorname{H}_{E}^{\#}
(resp.
s\in\Omega_{E}/p^{r+1-1/(p-1)}
) used in
loc.cit.
in order to avoid clashes with notation used previously in this article. The space
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#},s_{\operatorname{can}})
carries a left action of the group
\mathcal{T}_{r}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\left(1+p^{r+1-1/(p-1)}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}\right)
; since this group is abelian, we can (and do) view this as a right action.

Over
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{V}_{0}(\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#},s_{\operatorname{can}})
, one has a universal morphism
\rho\colon\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#}\to\mathcal{O}^{+}_{V}
which restricts to an isomorphism
\Omega_{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{V}
mapping the marked section
s_{\operatorname{can}}
to
1
modulo
p^{r+1-1/(p-1)}
. In particular, the kernel
\mathcal{U}
of the universal morphism
\rho
induces a decomposition
\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#}=\Omega_{E}\oplus\mathcal{U}
(and
\mathcal{U}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{V}
-module of rank one). We let
W\to V
denote the (right)
\mathbb{G}^{+}_{m}
-torsor parameterising bases
\{e,f\}
of
\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#}
with
e\in\Omega_{E}
,
f\in\mathcal{U}
, and
e
mapping to
1
under the universal morphism
\rho
. Here
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{+}(\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+}))=(R^{+})^{\times}
and it acts on the basis by rescaling the vector
f
by the
inverse
of the element in
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{+}
. Furthermore, the map
W\to V
is
\mathcal{T}_{r}^{\prime}
-equivariant, where the action on the source is given by rescaling
e
by the inverse of the element in
\mathcal{T}_{r}^{\prime}
. Finally, we equip
W
with an action of the group

\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathcal{T}_{r}^{\prime}&\\
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}&\mathbb{G}_{m}^{+}\end{matrix}\right)

by declaring
g\cdot\{e,f\}=\{ae,be+cf\}
for
{{}^{t}g^{-1}}=\left(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
&c\end{matrix}\right)
. Note that
W\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
is a
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\prime}
-torsor. By using the fact that
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{E}}\cong\left(\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#}[1/p]\right)^{\vee}
and the fact that
\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(E)^{\#}
has a canonical decomposition
\Omega_{E}\oplus\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{can}}
(where
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{can}}
denotes the unit root subsheaf) over the ordinary locus, one has morphisms

\mathcal{IG}\hookrightarrow W\hookrightarrow P_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}

with both maps defining reductions of structure. Therefore, by a similar argument as in Proposition
6.2.1
, one can identify
W
with
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}^{\operatorname{AI}}\times^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\operatorname{AI}}}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{r}^{\prime}
after possibly increasing
s
. We conclude that there are natural isomorphisms

\mathcal{V}_{r,s,(k;-k)}^{\operatorname{AI}}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}_{r}}((k;0),\pi_{W,*}\mathcal{O}_{W})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}_{r}^{\prime}}(k,\pi_{V,*}\mathcal{O}_{V})\cong\mathbb{W}_{k,I}[1/p].

Here
\pi_{W}\colon W\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
and
\pi_{V}\colon V\to\mathcal{X}_{s}
denote the structural maps; and we have used the fact that
-w_{0}(k;-k)=(k;0)
.

The result for general
\kappa=(k;w)
follows from the identification
\mathcal{V}_{r,s,\kappa}^{\operatorname{AI}}\cong\mathcal{V}_{r,s,(k;-k)}^{\operatorname{AI}}
arising from identifying
E
and
E^{D}
via the principal polarisation. Parts (2) and (3) follow from tracing through the definitions and constructions in §
5.1
, §
6.4
and
[
AI21
, §3.4, §3.6–§3.7]
(again, identifying
E
with its dual
E^{D}
).
∎

If we set
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{r,s}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}^{\operatorname{AI}},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{r,s}^{\operatorname{AI}}}\right)
, then we see that we have an inclusion

(6.7.3)

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}\subset\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}

and the inclusion
\mathscr{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}\subset\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
is an isomorphism on finite-slope parts (with respect to the action of
U_{p}
). The space
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
is much larger than
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}
though; the difference between the two spaces in (
6.7.3
) is that
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
incorporates “congruences between the subspace
\mathcal{U}
in the proof of Proposition
6.7.1
and the unit root subsheaf
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{can}}
” – roughly speaking, when restricting to the Igusa tower, the space
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
locally looks like the colimit over
n
of a number of copies of
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\frac{X-1}{p^{n}},\frac{Y}{p^{n}},\frac{Z-1}{p^{n}}\rangle
(see Proposition
5.2.1
), whereas
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}
looks like the colimit over
n
of a number of copies of
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\frac{X-1}{p^{n}},Y,\frac{Z-1}{p^{n}}\rangle
. This is the key difference which allows us to extend
\nabla
to an action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

It does not seem possible to improve the results of
[
AI21
]
without incorporating such congruences with the unit root subsheaf. More precisely, as indicated in
[
AI21
, Remark 3.39]
, in order to define an action of
\nabla^{s}
on
\mathscr{N}_{\kappa}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}
for a locally analytic character
s
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, it is necessary to assume that
s
and
\kappa
are analytic up to finite-order twist when restricted to
1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(in the language of
op.cit.
, this condition is written as
u_{s}\in\Lambda_{I_{s}}^{0}
and
u_{k}\in\Lambda_{I}^{0}
). The reason for this is due to the presence of the “unbounded denominators” in the displayed equation of
[
AI21
, Remark 3.39]
. In our definition of
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
, we allow the coordinate “
V=Y(1+pZ)^{-1}
” (with notation as in
loc.cit.
) to be arbitrarily divisible by
p
, which cancels out these problematic denominators.
5
5

5

One could also try to bound the action of
\binom{\nabla}{k}
on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}
in a similar way as in this article (by the formula in Lemma
4.3.2
for example), however one runs into the same issue with “unbounded denominators” (because the local coordinate
Y
is not arbitrarily divisible by powers of
p
in the definition of
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}
).

Remark 6.7.4
.

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\infty,\rho}}(N)
denote the space of nearly overconvergent modular forms defined in
[
Urb14
, §3.3.2]
(with notation as in
loc.cit.
). As explained in
[
AI21
, §B.2, p.2077]
, the construction of Andreatta–Iovita corresponds to the integral structure alluded to in
[
Urb14
, Remark 10]
; in particular, one has

\varinjlim_{\rho}\widehat{\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\infty,\rho}}(N)\cong\varinjlim_{s}\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathbb{W}_{k_{\mathfrak{U}}}[1/p])=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{AI}}_{\kappa_{\mathfrak{U}}}

where
k_{\mathfrak{U}}
denotes the universal character of
\mathfrak{U}
,
\kappa_{\mathfrak{U}}
is any locally analytic character of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
extending
k_{\mathfrak{U}}
, and
\mathbb{W}_{k_{\mathfrak{U}}}[1/p]
denotes the restriction of
\mathbb{W}_{k,I}[1/p]
to
\mathfrak{U}
(for any choice of interval
I=[0,p^{b}]
such that
\mathfrak{U}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}
, which always exists because
\mathfrak{U}
is assumed to be quasi-compact). We therefore see that the space
\widehat{\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\infty,\rho}}(N)
is also not large enough for
p
-adically interpolating the Gauss–Manin connection in the level of generality of this article (i.e., extending
\nabla
to an action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
) for the same reasons as above.

7.
p
-adic
L
-functions

In this final section, we describe an application of our theory to the construction of triple product and Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions in families, without the restriction on the weight imposed in
[
AI21
]
. Throughout, we let
p>2
and let
N\geq 4
be an integer which is prime to
p
. We take
K=\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K^{p}
to be the compact open subgroup of all matrices which lie in the upper-triangular unipotent modulo
N\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
.

We fix an isomorphism
\iota_{p}\colon{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
throughout, which gives a canonical choice of
N
-th root of unity
\zeta_{N}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\iota_{p}^{-1}(e^{2\pi i/N})
(after fixing
i=\sqrt{-1}
). Any
q
-expansion in this section will refer to the
q
-expansion at the cusp
(\operatorname{Tate}(q),\alpha_{N}^{\operatorname{can}})
, where
\operatorname{Tate}(q)
is the Tate curve over
\mathbb{Z}[1/N](\!(q)\!)
, and
\alpha_{N}^{\operatorname{can}}
is the canonical level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
-structure given by
\zeta_{N}
. Note that the map from overconvergent modular forms to
q
-expansions at this cusp is injective, as can be seen by using the
q
-expansion principle for
p
-adic modular forms (
[
Kat73
]
) and the inclusion of overconvergent modular forms into
p
-adic modular forms. Furthermore, we base-change everything in this section over a finite Galois extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
containing
\zeta_{N}
, but omit this from the notation.

Convention 7.0.1
.

As is customary in the literature, our weights will be locally analytic characters of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and not
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. This amounts to choosing a normalisation: for any locally analytic character
\kappa\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to R^{\times}
, we set

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{(\kappa;w(\kappa))}

where
w(\kappa)=-\kappa/2
(resp.
w(\kappa)=(1-\kappa)/2
) if
\kappa(-1)=1
(resp.
\kappa(-1)=-1
). These weight spaces are stable under the action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},R)
. We adopt similar notations for overconvergent modular forms.

Finally, we set
U_{p}^{\circ}=p^{-1}U_{p}
, which is the normalisation giving the usual description of the
U_{p}
-Hecke operator on
q
-expansions. All slope decompositions will be with respect to
U_{p}^{\circ}
.

7.1.
The eigencurve

Let
\mathcal{C}
denote the Buzzard–Coleman–Mazur cuspidal eigencurve (over
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
) of tame level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
, which comes equipped with a weight map
w\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{W}
, where
\mathcal{W}
denotes the weight space parameterising continuous characters on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Over
\mathcal{C}
, we have a universal eigenform

\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}=\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}q^{n}\quad\in\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{C})[\![q]\!]

with
a_{1}=1
and
a_{p}\in\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{C})^{\times}
, satisfying the following universal property: for any affinoid
U
with a weight morphism
\kappa:U\to\mathcal{W}
, and any family
\mathcal{F}_{U}
of finite slope eigenforms over
U
of tame level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
and weight
\kappa
, there exists a unique morphism
U\to\mathcal{C}
lifing
\kappa
such that
\mathcal{F}_{U}
is the pullback of
\mathcal{F}^{\rm univ}
(cf. the discussion just after
[
Loe18
, Definition 3.5]
). For any quasi-compact open affinoid
V\subset\mathcal{C}
, we let
\mathcal{F}_{V}\in\mathcal{O}(V)[\![q]\!]
denote the pullback of
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}
to
V
.

Let
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}\subset\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}
denote the subspace of cuspidal overconvergent modular forms, i.e. those which vanish at all the cusps (not just the specific choice above). This space is stable under the actions of
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
U_{p}
. Over
\mathcal{W}
we have a universal sheaf of cuspidal overconvergent modular forms
\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}
, such that for any quasi-compact open
U\subset\mathcal{W}
, we have
\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}(U)=\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}
(where
\kappa
is the universal character of
U
). We let
\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w^{*}\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}
denote the pullback to
\mathcal{C}
.

We have the following proposition:

Proposition 7.1.1
.

Let
V\subset\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-compact open affinoid subspace, and let
U=w(V)\subset\mathcal{W}
denote its image in weight space with universal character
\kappa\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(U)^{\times}
. Then there exists a “universal cohomology class”

\omega_{V}\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V)=\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}(U)}\mathcal{O}(V)

with
q
-expansion given by
\mathcal{F}_{V}
. Moreover the restriction of
\omega_{V}
to
\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V^{\prime})
is equal to
\omega_{V^{\prime}}
for
V^{\prime}\subset V
, so these classes glue to give a universal cohomology class
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C})
.

Proof.

This follows from the duality between cuspidal overconvergent forms and Hecke algebras (cf. for instance,
[
Urb14
, §4.2]
for trivial tame level, or
[
Col97
, Proposition B5.6]
). Observe that, since we are working at tame level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
, here we consider the Hecke algebra generated by the Hecke operators
T_{\ell}
for
\ell\nmid Np
,
U_{\ell}
and the diamond operators
\langle\ell\rangle
for
\ell\mid N
, and the Hecke operator
U_{p}
.
∎

7.1.1.
The dual class

Let
V\subset\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-compact open affinoid subspace, and let
\omega_{V}\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V)
denote the universal cohomology class provided by Proposition
7.1.1
. We will now explain how to associate a “dual class” to
\omega_{V}
. As explained in
[
AI21
, §5.2]
, one has an Atkin–Lehner involution
w_{N}
on
X=X_{1}(N)
(as
\zeta_{N}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
), which extends to involutions on
M_{\operatorname{dR}}
and
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}
, compatible with the morphism
\mathcal{IG}_{\infty}\to M_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
. This induces an involution
w_{N}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}
which commutes with the
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
U_{p}
actions and preserves
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}
.

Definition 7.1.2
.

Let
\omega_{V}^{c}
denote the “dual class” given by

\omega^{c}_{V}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w_{N}(\omega_{V})\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V).

These classes are compatible for
V^{\prime}\subset V
because the same is true for the Atkin–Lehner involution
w_{N}
, and we denote by
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C})
the dual class obtained by gluing.

7.1.2.
A linear functional

For any integer
M|N
, let
\mathcal{C}(M)
denote the Buzzard–Coleman–Mazur cuspidal eigencurve of tame level
\Gamma_{1}(M)
, and we let
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(M)
denote the space of cuspidal overconvergent modular forms of weight
\kappa_{1}
and tame level
\Gamma_{1}(M)
. If
M=N
, we omit this from the notation and we keep the notations of the previous sections.

Definition 7.1.3
.

We say that a point
x\in\mathcal{C}(M)
is:

•

classical
if
w(x)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and the specialisation
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x}
of the universal eigenform
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}
over
\mathcal{C}(M)
at the point
x
is the
q
-expansion of a normalised cuspidal modular form of weight
w(x)
and level
\Gamma_{1}(M)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p^{r})
, for some integer
r\geq 1
;

•

crystalline
if
x
is classical and the newform associated with the modular form
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x}
has level
\Gamma_{1}(M^{\prime})
for some
M^{\prime}|M
;

•

p
-regular crystalline
if
x
is crystalline and the roots of the Hecke polynomial at
p

X^{2}-a_{p}(f)X+\varepsilon_{f}(p)p^{w(x)-1}

are distinct, where
f
denotes the newform associated with
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x}
with nebentypus
\varepsilon_{f}
.

We make the following assumption:

Assumption 7.1.4
.

Let
U_{1}\subset\mathcal{W}
be a connected quasi-compact open affinoid subspace in weight space with universal character
\kappa_{1}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(U_{1})^{\times}
. We suppose that there exists an integer
N_{\mathcal{F}}
dividing
N
and an open
V_{1}\subset\mathcal{C}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
lying over
U_{1}
such that

•

The weight map
w\colon V_{1}\to U_{1}
is an isomorphism.

•

Every specialisation of the Coleman family
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}_{V_{1}}
at a classical point
x\in V_{1}
with
w(x)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
, is
noble
; i.e. it is the
p
-stabilisation of a normalised cuspidal newform of level
\Gamma_{1}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
that is
p
-regular, with the additional condition that its local Galois representation is not the sum of two characters if it is of critical slope (see
[
LZ16
, Definition 4.6.3]
).

Remark 7.1.5
.

Many such opens
V_{1}
exist by starting with a noble eigenform and taking a sufficiently small neighbourhood of its corresponding point in the eigencurve (see
[
Bel12
]
).

The Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
will constitute the first variable of the triple
p
-adic
L
-function.

Lemma 7.1.6
.

Let
\omega_{V_{1}}\in\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
denote the universal class over
V_{1}
, as in Proposition
7.1.1
. After possibly shrinking
V_{1}
, there exists a unique
\mathcal{O}(V_{1})
-linear Hecke equivariant (for the Hecke operators
\{T_{\ell}:\ell\nmid pN_{\mathcal{F}}\}\cup\{U_{p}\}
) map

(7.1.7)

\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})\to\mathcal{O}(V_{1})

sending the universal class
\omega_{V_{1}}
to
1
, where the action of the Hecke algebra on
\mathcal{O}(V_{1})
is through the Hecke eigensystem corresponding to the Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

If
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}
denotes the Hecke eigencharacter associated with the Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
, then
\omega_{V_{1}}
is an eigenclass for the Hecke action with eigencharacter
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}
. We claim that this appears as a direct summand in
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
with multiplicity one. Indeed, it suffices to show this in
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h}
for sufficiently large
h\in\mathbb{N}
, since the slope
\leq h
part is a direct summand of
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
. Let
\mathbb{T}_{h}=\mathbb{T}(\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h})
denote the Hecke algebra over
\mathcal{O}(U_{1})
(generated by the same Hecke operators as in the proof of Proposition
7.1.1
) acting faithfully on the module
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h}
.

Since the weight map induces an isomorphism on
V_{1}
(Assumption
7.1.4
), there exists an idempotent
e
in
\mathbb{T}_{h}
such that
e\mathbb{T}_{h}=\mathcal{O}(V_{1})\cong\mathcal{O}(U_{1})
and
e\mathbb{T}_{h}\subset\mathbb{T}_{h}
is the (generalised) eigenspace for the character
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}
. We note that we also have a stronger property, namely,
e\mathbb{T}_{h}\subset\mathbb{T}_{h}
is the (generalised) eigenspace for the action of the Hecke operators
\{T_{\ell}:\ell\nmid pN_{\mathcal{F}}\}\cup\{U_{p}\}
with eigencharacter
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}
. This is because any Coleman family over
U_{1}
with the same prime-to-
N_{\mathcal{F}}
Hecke eigensystem as
\mathcal{F}
must coincide with
\mathcal{F}
(this follows from the second bullet point in Assumption
7.1.4
).

Finally, by the duality between overconvergent modular forms and Hecke algebras (see the proof of Proposition
7.1.1
), the same properties are true for
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h}
, namely: one has
e\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h}\cong\mathcal{O}(U_{1})
and
e\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h}\subset\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})^{\leq h}
is the (generalised) eigenspace for the action of the Hecke operators
\{T_{\ell}:\ell\nmid pN_{\mathcal{F}}\}\cup\{U_{p}\}
with eigencharacter
\alpha_{\mathcal{F}}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We extend this to a linear functional on
\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}
as follows. For any divisor
a\geq 1
of
N/N_{\mathcal{F}}
, we have a finite étale morphism
[a]\colon X_{1}(N)\to X_{1}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
as described at the start of
[
AI21
, §5]
. This induces a trace morphism
[a]_{*}\colon\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
and a pullback map
[a]^{*}\colon\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}(N_{\mathcal{F}})\to\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}
, with the latter given by translation of the argument by
a
on classical modular forms.

Definition 7.1.8
.

Let
\underline{\mu}=(\mu_{a})_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}
be a tuple of elements of
L\cap\iota_{p}^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
indexed by the divisors of
N/N_{\mathcal{F}}
. We set
\omega_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ}}=\sum_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}\mu_{a}\cdot[a]^{*}(\omega_{V_{1}})\in\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}
. Set
\omega_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ}}^{c}=w_{N}(\omega_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ}})
. We define

\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}}\colon\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}\to\mathcal{O}(V_{1})

to be the
\mathcal{O}(V_{1})
-linear map sending a class
\delta\in\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{1}}
to the image of
[\Gamma_{1}(N_{\mathcal{F}}):\Gamma_{1}(N)]^{-1}\cdot\sum_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}\bar{\mu}_{a}\cdot[a]_{*}w_{N}(\delta)
under the map (
7.1.7
), where
\bar{\cdot}
denotes complex conjugation. This is Hecke equivariant away from
Np
and
U_{p}
-equivariant, where the Hecke action on the target is given by the eigensystem for
\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}
(see
[
Loe18
, Lemma 3.4]
).

We have the following specialisation formula:

Lemma 7.1.9
.

Let
x_{1}\in w^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2})\cap V_{1}
, let
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}
denote the specialisation of the Coleman family at
x_{1}
and let

\lambda^{\operatorname{cl}}_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c}}\colon S_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p);\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}

denote the restriction of the specialisation of
\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}}
at
x_{1}
to the space of classical cusp forms of weight
k\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w(x_{1})
and level
\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
(using
\iota_{p}
to extend scalars to
\mathbb{C}
).
6
6

6

Note that one has a natural map
\mathcal{X}_{r}\to\mathcal{X}_{0}(p)
(where the latter denotes the modular curve of level
\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
) which induces a Hecke equivariant map from cuspforms of level
\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
to cuspidal overconvergent modular forms (see
[
BP22
, §5.4.4]
).
Let
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c}=\mathcal{F}^{\circ}_{x_{1}}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}}^{-1}
, where
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}}
denotes the nebentypus of
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\circ}_{x_{1}}=\sum_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}\mu_{a}\cdot[a]^{*}\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}
. Then

\lambda^{\operatorname{cl}}_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c}}(g)=\frac{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c},g\rangle_{N,p}}{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{c},\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{c}\rangle_{N,p}}

for any
g\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p);\mathbb{C})
, where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{N,p}
denotes the Petersson inner product of level
\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
as in
[
DR14
, Eq. (35)]
(which is Hermitian linear in the first variable).

Proof.

The restriction of the specialisation of (
7.1.7
) at
x_{1}
to complex-valued cusp forms of weight
k
and level
\Gamma_{1}(N_{\mathcal{F}})\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
defines a Hecke equivariant map
S_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N_{\mathcal{F}})\cap\Gamma_{0}(p);\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}
factoring through the eigenspace associated with
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}
, and sending
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}
to
1
. Set
M=N_{\mathcal{F}}
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}
is a noble, this map must therefore be equal to

\frac{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}},-\rangle_{M,p}}{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}},\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}\rangle_{M,p}}=\frac{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}},-\rangle_{M,p}}{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{c},\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{c}\rangle_{M,p}}=[\Gamma_{1}(M):\Gamma_{1}(N)]\frac{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}},-\rangle_{M,p}}{\langle\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{c},\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{c}\rangle_{N,p}}.

Now one uses the fact that
[a]_{*}
(resp.
w_{N}
) and
[a]^{*}
(resp.
w_{N}
) are adjoint under the Petersson inner product, and that it is Hermitian linear in the first variable.
∎

7.2.
Triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions

We now construct a pairing over two copies of the eigencurve which will be used in our construction of triple product and Rankin-Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions. Fix a Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
over the open
V_{1}\subset\mathcal{C}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
(which is isomorphic to an open
U_{1}\subset\mathcal{W}
via the weight map) as in §
7.1.2
and Assumption
7.1.4
.

Notation 7.2.1
.

Let
\left(V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\right)^{+}\subset V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}
denote the open and closed subspace defined by the condition: for any
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in V_{1}({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})\times\mathcal{C}({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})\times\mathcal{C}({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})
, the weight

w(x_{1})-w(x_{2})-w(x_{3})

is even, i.e. there exists
u\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
such that
w(x_{1})-w(x_{2})-w(x_{3})=2u
.

Let
V_{2},V_{3}\subset\mathcal{C}
be two quasi-compact open affinoid subspaces with images
U_{2}=w(V_{2}),U_{3}=w(V_{3})\subset\mathcal{W}
and such that
V_{1}\times V_{2}\times V_{3}\subseteq(V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
. Let
a_{p}(\mathcal{F})
denote the coefficient of
q^{p}
in the series
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{V_{1}}
, and suppose that the
p
-adic Banach norm of
a_{p}(\mathcal{F})
is
\leq h
, for some integer
h\geq 0
. By shrinking
V_{i}
(
i=1,2,3
) if necessary we have the following:

•

For
i=1,2,3
, let
\kappa_{i}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(U_{i})^{\times}
denote the universal character of
U_{i}
and
\hat{\kappa}_{i}
its pullback to
U=U_{1}\times U_{2}\times U_{3}
. Then, we may assume that:

\hat{\kappa}_{1}-\hat{\kappa}_{2}-\hat{\kappa}_{3}

is even, i.e. there exists
u\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(U)^{\times}
such that
\hat{\kappa}_{1}-\hat{\kappa}_{2}-\hat{\kappa}_{3}=2u
.

•

The space of nearly overconvergent modular forms
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\hat{\kappa}_{1}}
has a slope
\leq h
decomposition and hence a
\mathcal{O}(U)
-linear overconvergent projector

\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\hat{\kappa}_{1}}\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,\leq h}_{\hat{\kappa}_{1}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}(F_{U_{1}}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{O}(U_{2})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{O}(U_{3}))

where
F_{U_{1}}
denotes the fraction ring of
U_{1}
(see Theorem
6.4.10
and Theorem
6.5.1
). Furthermore, the space of nearly overconvergent modular forms
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
over
\mathcal{O}(U)
comes equipped with an action

C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{O}(U))\times\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}

as in Theorem
2.3.2
, extending the Gauss–Manin connection. We denote this action by
\star
.

Definition 7.2.2
.

With notation as above, for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V_{2})
and
\psi\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V_{3})
, we define

\Xi(\phi,\psi)=\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h_{1}}((u\cdot 1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})\star\hat{\phi}\times\hat{\psi})\in\mathscr{S}^{\dagger,\leq h}_{\kappa_{1}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}(V_{1})}\left(F_{V_{1}}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{O}(V_{2})\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{O}(V_{3})\right)

where
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
denotes the indicator function of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, and
\hat{\phi}
and
\hat{\psi}
denote the pullback of the classes to
V_{1}\times V_{2}\times V_{3}
.

We have the following lemma:

Lemma 7.2.3
.

With notation as above:

(1)

The construction
\Xi(-,-)
is
\mathcal{O}(V_{2})
-linear in the first variable and
\mathcal{O}(V_{3})
-linear in the second variable.

(2)

If
V_{i}^{\prime}\subset V_{i}
(
i=2,3
) then
\Xi(-,-)
and
\Xi^{\prime}(-,-)
are compatible under restriction, where
\Xi^{\prime}(-,-)
denotes the construction in Definition
7.2.2
over
V_{1}\times V_{2}^{\prime}\times V_{3}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, the constructions are compatible for different choices of
h
.

(3)

The constructions glue to give a morphism of sheaves
7
7

7

For a pair of sheaves
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
of Banach modules on two spaces
X,Y
, we denote by
\mathcal{F}\;\hat{\boxtimes}\;\mathcal{G}
the sheaf on
X\times Y
obtained by sheafifying the assignment
U\times V\mapsto\mathcal{F}(U)\;\hat{\otimes}\;\mathcal{G}(V)
.
over
(V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}

{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{V_{1}}\;\hat{\boxtimes}\;\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}\;\hat{\boxtimes}\;\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}\to(\mathscr{S}^{\dagger,\leq h}_{\kappa_{1}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}(V_{1})}\mathcal{K}_{V_{1}})\;\hat{\boxtimes}\;\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}}\;\hat{\boxtimes}\;\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}}

where
\mathcal{K}_{V_{1}}
denotes the sheaf of meromorphic functions on
V_{1}
.

Proof.

The first part is clear and the third part follows from the second. Therefore, we just need to prove part (2). But this just follows from the linearity of
\star
and
\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}
, and the compatibility of the slope
\leq h
projectors as
h
varies.
∎

We consider the following pairing:

Definition 7.2.4
.

Fix a tuple
\underline{\mu}=(\mu_{a})_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}
as in Definition
7.1.8
. Then, for any pair of opens
V_{2},V_{3}\subset\mathcal{C}
(not necessarily quasi-compact nor affinoid) such that
V_{1}\times V_{2}\times V_{3}\subset(V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
, we consider the following bilinear pairing:

\displaystyle J_{\underline{\mu}}(-,-)\colon\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V_{2})\times\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V_{3})

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{Mer}(V_{1}\times V_{2}\times V_{3})

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle\mapsto\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}}\left({\boldsymbol{\Xi}}(\phi,\psi)\right)

where
\operatorname{Mer}(V_{1}\times V_{2}\times V_{3})
denotes the module of meromorphic functions on
V_{1}\times V_{2}\times V_{3}
. This pairing glues to a bilinear pairing
\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C})\times\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C})\to\operatorname{Mer}(V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
, which we continue to denote by
J_{\underline{\mu}}(-,-)
.

We define the universal triple product
p
-adic
L
-function as follows:

Definition 7.2.5
.

Fix a Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
over
V_{1}
satisfying Assumption
7.1.4
as in §
7.1.2
, and fix a tuple
\underline{\mu}=(\mu_{a})_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}
as in Definition
7.1.8
. We define
\mathscr{L}_{p}\in\operatorname{Mer}(V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
to be the meromorphic function given by

\mathscr{L}_{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}J_{\underline{\mu}}(\omega_{\mathcal{C}},\omega_{\mathcal{C}})

where
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
denotes the universal cohomology class of Proposition
7.1.1
.

7.2.1.
Interpolation property

We now describe the interpolation property for the universal triple product
p
-adic
L
-function. Throughout this section, fix a Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
over
V_{1}\subset\mathcal{C}(N_{\mathcal{F}})
as in §
7.1.2
and Assumption
7.1.4
, and let
U_{1}=w(V_{1})\subset\mathcal{W}
. Fix a tuple
\underline{\mu}=(\mu_{a})_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}
as in Definition
7.1.8
.

Definition 7.2.6
.

We say that a point
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in V_{1}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}
is
unbalanced crystalline
if:

•

x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}
are classical and
p
-regular crystalline (Definition
7.1.3
);

•

the weights
(w(x_{1}),w(x_{2}),w(x_{3}))
satisfy
w(x_{1})\geq w(x_{2})+w(x_{3})
and the nebentypes
\varepsilon_{f},\varepsilon_{g},\varepsilon_{h}
of the newforms
(f,g,h)
associated with
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})
satisfy
\varepsilon_{f}\cdot\varepsilon_{g}\cdot\varepsilon_{h}=1
.
8
8

8

This implies that there exists an integer
t\geq 0
such that
w(x_{1})-w(x_{2})-w(x_{3})=2t
.

We denote the set of unbalanced crystalline points by
\Sigma_{\operatorname{cris}}
(which depends on the fixed choice of
\mathcal{F}
).

Given an unbalanced crystalline point
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\Sigma_{\operatorname{cris}}
, we can fix the following data.

•

(Weights) We set
(k,\ell,m)=(w(x_{1}),w(x_{2}),w(x_{3}))
.

•

(
U_{p}^{\circ}
-eigenvalues) We let
(\alpha_{f},\alpha_{g},\alpha_{h})
denote the coefficients of
q^{p}
in
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x_{2}},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x_{3}}
respectively.

•

(Newforms) We let
(f,g,h)
denote the newforms of levels
\Gamma_{1}(N_{f})
,
\Gamma_{1}(N_{g})
,
\Gamma_{1}(N_{h})
, associated with
\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x_{2}},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x_{3}}
respectively. Note that, since the point
x
is unbalanced crystalline, all of the integers
N_{f}
,
N_{g}
,
N_{h}
divide
N
. Furthermore, by Assumption
7.1.4
, one has
N_{f}=N_{\mathcal{F}}
.

•

(Test data) We obtain a triple
(f^{\circ},g^{\circ},h^{\circ})
of cuspidal modular forms, where:
g^{\circ}
and
h^{\circ}
are the unique eigenforms of level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
such that
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x_{2}},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{univ}}_{x_{3}}
are the
p
-stabilisations of
g^{\circ}
,
h^{\circ}
with respect to the roots
\alpha_{g}
,
\alpha_{h}
; and we define
f^{\circ}=\sum_{a|N/N_{\mathcal{F}}}\mu_{a}\cdot[a]^{*}(f)
. Note that
\mathcal{F}^{\circ}_{x_{1}}
is the
p
-stabilisation of
f^{\circ}
at the root
\alpha_{f}
.

To state the interpolation property for
\mathscr{L}_{p}
, we need to introduce several Euler factors. Let
(\beta_{f},\beta_{g},\beta_{h})
denote the roots of the Hecke polynomials at
p
that are different from
(\alpha_{f},\alpha_{g},\alpha_{h})
. Set
c=(k+\ell+m-2)/2
and define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)

\displaystyle=(1-\beta_{f}\alpha_{g}\alpha_{h}p^{-c})(1-\beta_{f}\alpha_{g}\beta_{h}p^{-c})(1-\beta_{f}\beta_{g}\alpha_{h}p^{-c})(1-\beta_{f}\beta_{g}\beta_{h}p^{-c}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}(f)

\displaystyle=(1-\beta_{f}^{2}\varepsilon_{f}(p)^{-1}p^{1-k}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)

\displaystyle=(1-\beta_{f}^{2}\varepsilon_{f}(p)^{-1}p^{-k}).

For any integer
j\geq 0
, let
\delta_{j}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{d}{dz}+\frac{j}{z-\bar{z}}\right)
denote the Maass–Shimura differential operator acting on weight
j
nearly holomorphic modular forms. For any
t\geq 0
, we set
\delta_{j}^{t}=\delta_{j+2(t-1)}\circ\delta_{j+2(t-2)}\circ\cdots\circ\delta_{j}
. Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{N}
denote the Petersson inner product of level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
on (complex-valued) nearly holomorphic modular forms.

Theorem 7.2.7
.

Let
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\Sigma_{\operatorname{cris}}
with associated newforms
(f,g,h)
and test data
(f^{\circ},g^{\circ},h^{\circ})
. Then
x
is not a pole for
\mathscr{L}_{p}
and the specialisation of
\mathscr{L}_{p}
at
x
satisfies:

(7.2.8)

\mathscr{L}_{p}(x)=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{0}(f)\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}\cdot\frac{I(f^{\circ},g^{\circ},h^{\circ})}{\langle f,f\rangle_{N}}

where we view the left-hand side of (
7.2.8
) in
\mathbb{C}
via
\iota_{p}
, and
I(f^{\circ},g^{\circ},h^{\circ})=\langle(f^{\circ})^{*},\delta_{\ell}^{t}g^{\circ}\times h^{\circ}\rangle_{N}
with
(f^{\circ})^{*}(\tau)=\overline{f^{\circ}(-\bar{\tau})}
.

Proof.

The proof of this is very similar to
[
DR14
]
and
[
AI21
]
. Let
\omega_{g^{\circ}}^{\alpha}
and
\omega_{h^{\circ}}^{\alpha}
denote the specialisations of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
at
x_{2}
and
x_{3}
respectively, and recall
t=(k-\ell-m)/2
. Firstly, we note that
\mathscr{L}_{p}(x)=J_{\underline{\mu}}(\omega_{\mathcal{C}},\omega_{\mathcal{C}})_{x}
is equal to

\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}_{x_{1}}}(\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}\left((x^{t}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})\star\omega_{g^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\times\omega_{h^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\right))=\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}_{x_{1}}}(e_{f^{\circ,c},\alpha}\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}\left((x^{t}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})\star\omega_{g^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\times\omega_{h^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\right)).

where
e_{f^{\circ,c},\alpha}\colon\mathscr{S}^{\dagger,\leq h}_{k}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{S}^{\dagger,\leq h}_{k}[\pi_{f^{*}},U_{p}^{\circ}=\varepsilon_{f}(p)^{-1}\alpha_{f}]
is the projection to the eigenspace for
(f^{\circ,\alpha})^{c}
(for the Hecke operators away from
Np
and
U_{p}
). Here
f^{\circ,\alpha}=\mathcal{F}^{\circ}_{x_{1}}
and
\pi_{f^{*}}
denotes the automorphic representation associated with
f^{*}=f\otimes\varepsilon_{f}^{-1}
. Set
\nu=(x^{t}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})\star\omega_{g^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\times\omega_{h^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{k}
. This class has the same
q
-expansion as the class

\nu^{\prime}=\nabla^{t}\omega^{\alpha,[p]}_{g^{\circ}}\times\omega_{h^{\circ}}^{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Fil}_{t}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{k}

where
(-)^{[p]}
is
p
-depletion. Any filtered piece
\operatorname{Fil}_{b}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{k}
injects into the space of
p
-adic modular forms (and hence the space of
q
-expansions), and since
\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}
is
U_{p}^{\circ}
-equivariant, and
\alpha_{f}\neq 0
, we therefore see that
\mathscr{L}_{p}(x)
is equal to

\mathscr{L}_{p}(x)=\lambda_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c}}(e_{f^{\circ,c},\alpha}\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}\nu^{\prime}).

By the computations in the proof of Lemma 5.9 and Lemma 5.10 in
[
AI21
]
, we see that

e_{f^{\circ,c},\alpha}\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}\nu^{\prime}=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}e_{f^{\circ,c},\alpha}\Pi^{\operatorname{oc},\leq h}(\nabla^{t}\omega_{g^{\circ}}\times\omega_{h^{\circ}})

where
\omega_{g^{\circ}}\in\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{\ell}
,
\omega_{h^{\circ}}\in\mathscr{S}^{\dagger}_{m}
denote the classes attached to
g^{\circ}
and
h^{\circ}
. But the argument in the right-hand side is classical: if we denote
\nu^{\prime\prime}=e_{f^{\circ,c}}\Pi^{\operatorname{hol}}(\delta_{\ell}^{t}g^{\circ}\times h^{\circ})
the projection to the
f^{\circ,c}
-eigenspace of the holomorphic projection of
\delta_{\ell}^{t}g^{\circ}\times h^{\circ}
, then we have

\mathscr{L}_{p}(x)=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}\lambda_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c}}((\nu^{\prime\prime})^{\alpha})

where
(\nu^{\prime\prime})^{\alpha}
is the
p
-stabilisation of
\nu^{\prime\prime}
with respect to the eigenvalue
\varepsilon_{f}(p)^{-1}\alpha_{f}
. By the specialisation property in Lemma
7.1.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{p}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}\lambda_{\mathcal{F}_{x_{1}}^{\circ,c}}((\nu^{\prime\prime})^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}\frac{\langle(f^{\circ,c})^{\alpha},(\nu^{\prime\prime})^{\alpha}\rangle_{N,p}}{\langle(f^{c})^{\alpha},(f^{c})^{\alpha}\rangle_{N,p}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{0}(f)\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}\frac{\langle f^{\circ,c},\nu^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{N}}{\langle f^{c},f^{c}\rangle_{N}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{E}(f,g,h)}{\mathcal{E}_{0}(f)\mathcal{E}_{1}(f)}\frac{\langle(f^{\circ})^{*},\delta_{\ell}^{t}g^{\circ}\times h^{\circ}\rangle_{N}}{\langle f,f\rangle_{N}}

where the third equality follows from an explicit calculation (c.f.
[
AI21
, Lemma 5.12]
) and we’ve used the fact that
(f^{\circ})^{*}=f^{\circ,c}
because the conductor of
\varepsilon_{f}
is not divisible by
p
.
∎

7.2.2.
Relation to central
L
-values

The trilinear period in Theorem
7.2.7
is closely related to central critical
L
-values of the Garrett–Rankin triple product
L
-function. More precisely:

Theorem 7.2.9

(
[
DR14
, Theorem 4.2]
)
.

There exists a constant
C
(defined over the number field generated by the Fourier coefficients of
f,g,h
and depending on
f^{\circ}
,
g^{\circ}
,
h^{\circ}
) such that

|I(f^{\circ},g^{\circ},h^{\circ})|^{2}=\frac{C}{\pi^{2k}}L(f,g,h,c)

where
c=(k+\ell+m-2)/2
and
L(f,g,h,-)
is the Garrett–Rankin triple product
L
-function.

Therefore, one can view
\mathscr{L}_{p}
as a universal “square-root” triple product
L
-function. Unfortunately, it does not seem possible (with the current methods) to construct a three-variable
p
-adic
L
-function over
(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
as we don’t know if the linear functional
\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}}
globalises. Furthermore, it can happen that the constant
C
is equal to zero for the test vectors
(f^{\circ},g^{\circ},h^{\circ})
(for example, if
N_{\mathcal{F}}=N
and there exists a prime dividing
N
, but not
N_{g}\cdot N_{h}
– see
[
DR14
, Remark 4.3]
). One has more freedom for choosing test vectors so that this constant is non-zero in small opens of
\mathcal{C}
, however we are not sure if this is possible globally.

7.3.
Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions

We close with an application to the construction of Rankin-Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions in three variables. Our strategy essentially follows the one in
[
AI21
, Appendix B]
circumventing the restriction on the weight space, by replacing
[
AI21
, Theorem 4.3]
by our generalised version Theorem
2.3.2
as the input into the construction. Let us give a very brief sketch of this construction.

Fix a Coleman family
\mathcal{F}
as in §
7.1.2
and Assumption
7.1.4
, defined over a quasi-compact affinoid open subspace
U_{1}\subset\mathcal{W}
. We first observe that Definition
7.2.4
still makes sense for non cuspidal forms
\psi
.

Definition 7.3.1
.

Fix a tuple
\underline{\mu}
as in Definition
7.1.8
. For any open subspaces
U_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W},V_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
such that
V_{1}\times U_{2}\times V_{3}\subseteq(V_{1}\times\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
, we define a bilinear pairing

\displaystyle J_{\underline{\mu}}(-,-):\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}(U_{2})\times\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(V_{3})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mer}(V_{1}\times U_{2}\times V_{3})

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathcal{F}^{\circ,c}}(\Xi(\phi,\psi)).

in exactly the same way as in Definition
7.2.4
, where
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}
denotes the sheaf over
\mathcal{W}
of overconvergent modular forms. This pairing glues to a pairing
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W})\times\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{C})\to\operatorname{Mer}(V_{1}\times\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
which we continue to denote by
J_{\underline{\mu}}(-,-)
.

This bilinear pairing can be used to construct three-variable Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions, by taking
\phi
to be a
p
-adic family of Eisenstein series. More precisely following
[
Loe18
, Lemma 3.2]
, there exists a
p
-adic family of Eisenstein series
E^{[p]}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W})
with
q
-expansion

E^{[p]}_{\kappa}(q)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 1\\
(n,p)=1\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{d|n}d^{\kappa-1}(\zeta_{N}^{d}+(-1)^{\kappa}\zeta_{N}^{-d})\right)q^{n}

where
\kappa
denotes the universal character. Recall that
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C})
is the universal class over the eigencurve.

Definition 7.3.2
.

We define the universal Rankin-Selberg
p
-adic
L
-function to be the meromorphic function on
(V_{1}\times\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{C})^{+}
given by

\mathscr{L}_{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}J_{\underline{\mu}}(E^{[p]}_{\kappa},\omega_{\mathcal{C}}).

Theorem 7.3.3
.

We have the following interpolation property: for any integer
k_{2}\geq 2
and noble classical points
x_{1}\in V_{1}
,
x_{3}\in\mathcal{C}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that
k_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w(x_{1})=k_{2}+w(x_{3})+2t
for some
t\geq 0
, one has

\mathscr{L}_{p}(x_{1},k_{2},x_{3})=(\star)\cdot L^{\operatorname{imp}}(f,h,k_{1}-1-t)

for some explicitly computable factor
(\star)
, where

•

L^{\operatorname{imp}}(f,h,s)
denotes the imprimitive Rankin–Selberg
L
-function as in
[
Loe18
, Definition 2.1]
.

•

f
(resp.
h
) is the newform associated with the specialisation of
\mathcal{F}
at
x_{1}
(resp. the eigenform corresponding to the point
x_{3}
).

Proof.

As in Theorem
7.2.7
, we can express classical specialisations of
\mathscr{L}_{p}
as a Petersson inner product between
(f^{\circ})^{*}
and
\delta_{k_{2}}^{t}E^{[p]}_{k_{2}}\cdot h^{\circ}
; the interpolation formula then follows from the calculations in
[
Loe18
]
.
∎

Remark 7.3.4
.

As in §
7.2.2
, the factor
(\star)
depends on the test data
f^{\circ}
and
h^{\circ}
. Furthermore, we note that this three variable
p
-adic
L
-function was essentially constructed in
[
Loe18
]
, but its construction relies on the
p
-adic variation of Beilinson–Flach Euler system classes in
[
LZ16
]
and imposes an ordinarity assumption on one of the families of overconvergent cuspidal forms. Our construction is completely independent of Euler systems.

Appendix A
Classical nearly holomorphic modular forms

In this appendix we describe the relation between
(\mathfrak{g},P)
-representations and
\mathcal{D}
-modules on Shimura varieties. We note that similar constructions can be found in
[
Liu19
]
.

A.1.
Preliminaries on the flag variety

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero. Let
G
be a reductive group over
k
and
P\subset G
a parabolic subgroup. Let
\operatorname{FL}=G/P
be the corresponding partial flag variety. It carries an action of
G
by left translation.

A.1.1.
G
-equivariant sheaves

We let
\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})
be the category of
G
-equivariant quasi-coherent sheaves over
\operatorname{FL}
. We let
\mathrm{Rep}(P)
be the category of algebraic representations of
P
on
k
-vector spaces. We define a functor:

\displaystyle F:\mathrm{Rep}(P)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=F(V)

as follows. The group
G
acts on itself by left and right translation. It follows that
\mathcal{O}_{G}
carries two
G
-actions, denoted
\star_{l}
and
\star_{r}
, given by the rule
g\star_{l}f(-)=f(g^{-1}\cdot-)
and
g\star_{r}f(-)=f(-\cdot g)
. We consider the projection
\pi:G\mapsto G/P
. For
(V,\rho)
an object of
\mathrm{Rep}(P)
, we let

F(V)=(\pi_{\star}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{k}V)^{P},

where the action of
P
on the tensor product is the diagonal action given by
\star_{r}
on the left factor and by
\rho
on the right factor. The action
\star_{l}
provides a
G
-equivariant structure on
F(V)
.

On the other hand, consider
1\cdot P=1\in\operatorname{FL}
, and let
i_{1}:\{1\}\rightarrow\operatorname{FL}
be the inclusion. Let
\mathcal{V}\in\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})
. The stalk at
1\in\operatorname{FL}
, denoted
i_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{V}
, is an
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL},1}
-module with a semi-linear action of
P
. The maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL},1}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL},1}
is stable under the action of
P
and the fibre at
1\in\operatorname{FL}
, denoted
i_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}
, is a
k
-linear representation of
P
. Hence we obtain a pullback functor:

\displaystyle i_{1}^{*}:\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}(P)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle i_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}.

Proposition A.1.1
.

The functors
F
and
i_{1}^{*}
are equivalences of categories, quasi-inverse of each other.

Example A.1.2
.

(1)

If
V\in\mathrm{Rep}(G)
, one can check that
F(V)=\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes_{k}V
.

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be the Lie algebras of
P\subset G
. We let
\mathfrak{p}^{0}=F(\mathfrak{p})\subseteq F(\mathfrak{g})=\mathfrak{g}^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{FL}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
. The tangent sheaf on
\operatorname{FL}
is given by
T_{\operatorname{FL}}=F(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})=\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\mathfrak{p}^{0}
.

(3)

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
be the sheaf of differential operators over
\operatorname{FL}
. This is an object of
\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})
. There is a surjective map
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
inducing an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})/(\mathfrak{p}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. One thus checks that
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}=F(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{p})}k)
.

A.1.2.
G
-equivariant
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-modules

As above, let
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
be the sheaf of differential operators over
\operatorname{FL}
. We let
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G}
be the category of
G
-equivariant
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-modules. Its objects are objects of
\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})
together with an action of
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
which is compatible with the
G
-equivariant structure.

We let
\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))
be the category of
k
-vector spaces
V
equipped with an algebraic representation of
P
, and a representation of
\mathfrak{g}
satisfying the following compatibility:

(1)

For any
v\in V,g\in\mathfrak{g}
and
p\in P
, we have
(\mathrm{Ad}(p)g)v=pgp^{-1}v
,

(2)

The action of
P
induces an action of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}
. This action coincides with the restriction to
\mathfrak{p}
of the action of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Lemma A.1.3
.

The functor
F
can be enriched to a functor:

\displaystyle F:\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G}

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=F(V)

Proof.

Let
(V,\rho)
be an object of
\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))
(where
\rho
stands for the action of both
P
and
\mathfrak{g}
).
We first observe that the
G
-equivariant structure induces an action
\star_{l}
of
\mathfrak{g}
on
F(V)
by derivations. This action can be extended linearly to an action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
which extends the action by derivations on
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. However it will not in general factor through an action of
T_{\operatorname{FL}}
on
F(V)
and induce a
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-module structure.

Instead, we construct a second action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
F(V)
by using the action
\rho
of
\mathfrak{g}
on
V
as follows. Let
f\in\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes V
. We let
h\in\mathfrak{g}
and define
h\star f=[g\mapsto g^{-1}hg\star_{\rho}f(g)]
. We check that this descends to an action on
F(V)
as follows. For
p\in P
we have:

\displaystyle p(\star_{r}\otimes\rho)(h\star f)(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(h\star f)(gp)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pp^{-1}g^{-1}hgpf(gp)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\star(p(\star_{r}\otimes\rho)f)(g).

We check that this action is
G
-equivariant as follows. For
t
in
G
we have

\displaystyle t\cdot h\star(t\star_{l}f)(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle tht^{-1}\star f(t^{-1}g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{-1}tht^{-1}gf(t^{-1}g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t\star_{l}(h\star f)(g).

The
\star
action is
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-linear.
The difference
\star_{\mathcal{D}}=\star_{l}-\star
defines an action by derivations of
\mathfrak{g}
on
F(V)
which extends the action by derivations on
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
.
We claim that the action
\star_{l}-\star
induces a
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-module structure on
F(V)
.
The main step is to check that
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
acts trivially. As the restriction of the action
\star_{\mathcal{D}}
of
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-linear and
G
-equivariant it suffices to check
that the induced action is trivial on
i_{1}^{*}
. The action
\star_{l}
restricts to the natural action of
P
on
V
via
i_{1}^{*}
and induces an action of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}
. The action
\star
restricts by construction to the natural action of
\mathfrak{p}
on
V
, induced by the inclusion
\mathfrak{p}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
. By the compatibility imposed on both actions, the difference is the trivial action of
\mathfrak{p}
.
∎

Lemma A.1.4
.

The functor
i_{1}^{*}
can be enriched to a functor

\displaystyle i_{1}^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle i_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be an object of
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G}
. The action of
G
induces an action
\star_{l}
of
\mathfrak{g}
. The
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-module action induces an action
\star_{\mathcal{D}}
of
\mathfrak{g}
. Both actions extend the action by derivations on
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
, therefore the difference is an
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-linear action which induces an action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
i_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}
.
∎

We immediately obtain the following proposition.

Proposition A.1.5
.

The categories
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G}
and
\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))
are equivalent via the quasi-inverse functors
i_{1}^{*}
and
F
.

Example A.1.6
.

(1)

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(P)
has a left adjoint
V\mapsto\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{p})}V
. Similarly, the forgetful functor
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{QCoh}_{G}(\operatorname{FL})
has a left adjoint
\mathcal{V}\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{V}
.
This implies that for any
\mathcal{V}=F(V)
with
V\in\mathrm{Rep}(P)
, one has
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{V}=F(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{p})}V)
.

(2)

Let
E,G\in\mathrm{Rep}(P)
be finite dimensional representations. Let
\mathcal{E}=F(E)
,
\mathcal{G}=F(G)
. Let
\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{G})
be the space of
G
-equivariant differential operators
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
. By definition, a
G
-equivariant differential operator is a global
G
-invariant section of

\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
, or equivalently a

G
-equivariant map

\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
. We deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Rep}(P)}(F^{\vee},\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{p})}E^{\vee})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g},P))}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{p})}F^{\vee},\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{p})}E^{\vee}).

A.1.3.
The case of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}

In this subsection, we freely use the notation in §
1.1
. Let
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}\subset\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
denote the lower triangular nilpotent Lie algebra, and let
w_{0}
denote the longest Weyl element of
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. We will consider certain
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{B})
-representations which belong to category
\mathcal{O}
. Since we are interested in representations of the lower triangular Borel subgroup (but define the positive roots to be those which lie in
B
) our conventions are slightly different from those found in the literature. For example, our convention is that Verma modules are
lowest
weight modules.

Definition A.1.7
.

For any
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
, the Verma module of lowest weight
w_{0}\kappa
is
M_{\kappa}=\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\overline{\mathfrak{b}})}w_{0}\kappa
. We let
M_{\kappa}^{\vee}
be its dual in category
\mathcal{O}
.
9
9

9

Here is how duality is defined. Let
M\in\mathcal{O}
. Then
M=\oplus_{\chi\in X^{*}(T)_{\mathbb{Q}}}M_{\chi}
. We let
M^{\vee}=\oplus_{\chi\in X^{*}(T)_{\mathbb{Q}}}M_{\chi}^{\vee}\subset\mathrm{Hom}(M,\mathbb{Q})
. The action on
M^{\vee}
is given by
gf(-)=f({{}^{t}g}\cdot-)
.

If
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)^{+}
, let
V_{\kappa}
be the irreducible representation of
G
of highest weight
\kappa
. Then there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow V_{\kappa}\rightarrow M^{\vee}_{\kappa}\rightarrow M_{w_{0}\kappa-2\rho}\rightarrow 0.

If
\kappa\notin X^{*}(T)^{+}
then
M^{\vee}_{\kappa}=M_{\kappa}
is irreducible.
The character of
M_{\kappa}^{\vee}
is
\sum_{n\geq 0}w_{0}\kappa+2n\rho
and we have a filtration
M_{\kappa}^{\vee}=\bigcup_{r\geq 0}\mathrm{Fil}_{r}M_{\kappa}^{\vee}
where
\mathrm{Fil}_{r}M_{\kappa}^{\vee}=M_{\kappa}^{\vee}[\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{r+1}]
is the subspace of elements killed by
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{r+1}
. Moreover, there is an isomorphism of
\overline{B}
-modules:
