M_{\kappa}^{\vee}[\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{r+1}]\cong\mathrm{Sym}^{r}\operatorname{St}\otimes\mathbb{Q}(w_{0}\kappa+r(1;-1))

where
\operatorname{St}
denotes the standard representation of
G
, and
\mathbb{Q}(w_{0}\kappa+r(1;-1))
is the one-dimensional line on which
\overline{B}
acts through the character
w_{0}\kappa+r(1;-1)
.

We let
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
be the space of functions on
\overline{B}
. We equip it with an action of
\overline{B}
via
b\star_{l}f(-)=f(b^{-1}\cdot-)
. We can turn it into a
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{B})
-module as follows. We have an open immersion
\overline{B}\hookrightarrow G/U
, where
U
denotes the upper triangular unipotent, and the left translation action of
G
on
G/U
induces an action of
\mathfrak{g}
stabilising
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
. We also have another action of
\overline{B}
via
b\star_{r}f(-)=f(-\cdot b)
.

We let
x,t_{1},t_{2}
be coordinates on
\overline{B}
, where the universal element of
\overline{B}
is written as

\begin{pmatrix}t_{2}&0\\
x&t_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Using this description, we have
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}=k[x,t_{1},t_{2},t_{1}^{-1},t_{2}^{-1}]
and the actions
\star_{r}
and
\star_{l}
of
\overline{B}
and that of
\mathfrak{g}
are easy to describe. Indeed, the infinitesimal action is given by

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\mapsto xt_{1}t_{2}^{-1}\partial_{t_{1}}-x\partial_{t_{2}},\quad\quad\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\mapsto-\partial_{t_{2}}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\mapsto-(\partial_{x}+\partial_{t_{1}}),\quad\quad\;\quad\quad\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\mapsto-t_{2}\partial_{x}.

The action of the lower triangular unipotent
\overline{U}
via
\star_{r}
or
\star_{l}
induces a filtration
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}=\bigcup_{r\geq 0}\mathrm{Fil}_{r}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}})
where
\mathrm{Fil}_{r}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}})
is the sub-module of vectors killed by
\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{r+1}
. This is the subspace of
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
of elements with degree in
x
bounded by
r
. The action of
T
via
\star_{r}
commutes with the actions of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\overline{B}
via
\star_{l}
and the space
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
decomposes as a sum of eigenspaces:

Lemma A.1.8
.

Via the action of
T
by
\star_{r}
, the space
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
decomposes as
\oplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)}(M_{\kappa})^{\vee}
with
(M_{\kappa})^{\vee}=\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}[-w_{0}\kappa]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(-w_{0}\kappa,\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}})
.

Let
\pi:G\rightarrow G/\overline{B}
be the canonical
\overline{B}
-torsor. It is a
G
-equivariant torsor via the left
G
-action.

Proposition A.1.9
.

The
\overline{B}
-torsor structure on
G
induces a decomposition,

\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}=\oplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)}\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]=\oplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)}\bigcup_{r\geq 0}\mathrm{Fil}_{r}\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]

where
\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]=F(M_{\kappa}^{\vee})
and
\mathrm{Fil}_{r}\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]=F(\mathrm{Fil}_{r}M_{\kappa}^{\vee})
. We have a
G
-equivariant connection
\nabla:\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\rightarrow\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/\mathbb{Q}}
inducing a connection:

\nabla_{\kappa}:\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/\mathbb{Q}}.

Moreover, we have
\nabla_{\kappa}(\mathrm{Fil}_{r}\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa])\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}_{r+1}\mathcal{O}_{G}[-w_{0}\kappa]\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/\mathbb{Q}}
for all
r\geq 0
.

Proof.

We have an isomorphism of
G
-equivariant sheaves
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}=F((\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}},\star_{l}))
, where
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
is equipped with the
\star_{l}
-action of
\overline{B}
. The
\star_{r}
-action of
\overline{B}
corresponds to the
\overline{B}
-torsor structure. All the properties can be read from the properties of
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
.
∎

A.2.
Shimura varieties

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum, and let
\{S_{K}\}_{K\subseteq G(\mathbb{A}_{f})}
be the tower of Shimura varieties, defined over the reflex field
E
. We let
P=P_{\mu}^{\operatorname{std}}
be the parabolic attached to (a representative of) the cocharacter
\mu
of the Shimura datum and let
\operatorname{FL}=G/P
be the flag variety, defined over
E
. We let
Z(G)
be the center of
G
and denote by
Z_{s}(G)
the largest subtorus which is
\mathbb{R}
-split but has no subtorus split over
\mathbb{Q}
. Set
G^{c}=G/Z_{s}(G)
,
P^{c}=P/Z_{s}(G)
, and define
M^{c}=M/Z_{s}(G)
where
M
is the Levi of
P
. Note
\operatorname{FL}=G^{c}/P^{c}
.

Let
G_{\operatorname{dR},K}\rightarrow S_{K}
denote the de Rham
G^{c}
-torsor (see
[
Mil90
, §III.3]
) and
P_{\operatorname{dR},K}\rightarrow S_{K}
its
P^{c}
-reduction. We define
M_{\operatorname{dR},K}=P_{\operatorname{dR},K}\times^{P^{c}}M^{c}
to be the push out of the
P^{c}
-torsor
P_{\operatorname{dR},K}
to an
M^{c}
-torsor
M_{\operatorname{dR},K}
. We have a diagram:

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G_{\operatorname{dR},K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{S_{K}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{FL}}

characterised by the property that the pullback of the
P^{c}
-torsor
G^{c}\rightarrow\operatorname{FL}
via
q
is
P_{\operatorname{dR},K}\rightarrow G_{\operatorname{dR},K}
.
We obtain a functor:

\displaystyle\operatorname{VB}_{K}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}_{G^{c}}(\operatorname{FL})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{QCoh}(S_{K})

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{0}(G^{c},p_{*}q^{*}\mathcal{V})

Lemma A.2.2
.

We have
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/E})=\Omega^{1}_{S_{K}/E}
and
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}})=\mathcal{D}_{S_{K}}
.

Proof.

The first statement
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/E})=\Omega^{1}_{S_{K}/E}
follows from Kodaira–Spencer theory. Let us recall the argument.
Recall that
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie algebra of
G
, and
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
is the associated vector bundle with flat connection over
\operatorname{FL}
. It carries a filtration
\mathfrak{n}^{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{0}
, where
\mathfrak{m}^{0}=\mathfrak{p}^{0}/\mathfrak{n}^{0}
is associated to the Levi
\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\mathfrak{p}^{0}
is the tangent sheaf. We explain how we can recover the isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\mathfrak{p}^{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
from the connection.

Passing to dual vector bundles, and using Griffiths transversality and the connection, we obtain a map
\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee}\rightarrow\mathfrak{m}^{0,\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}
or equivalently a map
\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathfrak{m}^{0}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}
. This map factors through the isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{1}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}
via the adjoint action of
\mathfrak{m}
on
\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}
which induces a map
\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathfrak{m}^{0}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee}
. The vector bundle
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee})
carries an integrable connection, and we thus get a map

\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S_{K}}}\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathfrak{m}^{0})\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{S_{K}/E}

which factors through the isomorphism
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathfrak{g}^{0,\vee}/\mathfrak{n}^{0,\vee})\cong\Omega^{1}_{S_{K}/E}
.

We now turn to the identification
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}})=\mathcal{D}_{S_{K}}
. We first observe that by the first point we have a map
\mathcal{O}_{S_{K}}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{S_{K}}\rightarrow\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}})
. We will show that
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}})
carries an algebra structure, and is generated by
\mathcal{O}_{S_{K}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{S_{K}}
subject to the usual relations. We recall that if
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
is an étale map of
E
-schemes then the pullback map
f^{*}\Omega^{1}_{Y/E}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X/E}
is an isomorphism. Similarly, the natural map
\mathcal{D}_{X}\rightarrow f^{*}\mathcal{D}_{Y}
is an isomorphism.

The
G^{c}
-torsor
G_{\operatorname{dR},K}\rightarrow S_{K}
has sections étale locally. Let
U\rightarrow S_{K}
be an étale map and let
s:U\rightarrow G_{\operatorname{dR},K}
be a section. By a deformation theory argument, one can choose
s
so that the map
q\circ s:U\rightarrow\operatorname{FL}
is étale. We deduce that
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E})|_{U}=(q\circ s)^{*}\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}=\mathcal{D}_{U/E}
.
We therefore obtain an algebra structure on
\operatorname{VB}_{K}(\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E})|_{U}
. This algebra structure glues using the
G^{c}
-equivariant action. Indeed, let
s^{\prime}:U\rightarrow G_{\operatorname{dR},K}
be another section. Then there exists
g\in G^{c}(U)
such that
s^{\prime}=g\circ s
. Let
\operatorname{act}:G^{c}\times\operatorname{FL}\rightarrow\operatorname{FL}
be the action map and let
p:G^{c}\times\operatorname{FL}\rightarrow\operatorname{FL}
be the projection. We have a map
U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(g,q\circ s)}}{{\rightarrow}}G^{c}\times\operatorname{FL}
and
\operatorname{act}((g,q\circ s))=q\circ s^{\prime}
, while
p((g,q\circ s))=q\circ s
. The equivariant structure on

\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}
is an isomorphism of algebras (satisfying a certain cocycle condition)
\operatorname{act}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}\xrightarrow{\sim}p^{*}\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}
. We thus get an isomorphism:
(g,q\circ s)^{*}\operatorname{act}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}\xrightarrow{\sim}(g,q\circ s)^{*}p^{*}\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}/E}
.
∎

We immediately obtain the following corollary:

Corollary A.2.3
.

The functor
\operatorname{VB}_{K}
induces a functor
\operatorname{VB}_{K}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mathrm{-Mod}_{G^{c}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{S_{K}}\mathrm{-Mod}
.

Using the equivalences of categories in §
A.1
, we have functors (that we keep denoting by
\operatorname{VB}_{K}
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}(P^{c})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{QCoh}(S_{K})

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g}^{c},P^{c}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{S_{K}}\mathrm{-Mod}

Let
S_{K,\Sigma}^{\operatorname{tor}}
be a toroidal compactification, where, as usual,
\Sigma
denotes a cone decomposition. These functors can be extended to functors
\operatorname{VB}^{\operatorname{can}}_{K}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}(P^{c})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{QCoh}(S^{\operatorname{tor}}_{K,\Sigma})

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}((\mathfrak{g}^{c},P^{c}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{S^{\operatorname{tor}}_{K,\Sigma}}\mathrm{-Mod}

where
\mathcal{D}_{S^{\operatorname{tor}}_{K,\Sigma}}
is the sheaf of logarithmic differential operators.

A.3.
Application to modular curves

We now specialise to the setting of modular curves, where
(G,X)=(\mathrm{GL}_{2},\mathcal{H})
with
\mathcal{H}
the upper and lower half-plane. We change notation and let
X_{K}=S_{K,\Sigma}^{\operatorname{tor}}
. We take a representative of
P
to be the lower triangular Borel
\overline{B}
with Levi
M=T
the diagonal torus.

A.3.1.
Modular forms

For any
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
, we let
\omega^{\kappa}_{K}
or simply
\omega^{\kappa}
be
\operatorname{VB}^{\operatorname{can}}_{K}(w_{0}\kappa)
. The space of weight
\kappa
, level
K
modular forms is
\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{K},\omega^{\kappa})
.

Remark A.3.1
.

Let
E\rightarrow X_{K}
be the universal semi-abelian scheme. Let
\omega_{E}
be the conormal sheaf, and
\mathrm{Lie}(E)
the Lie algebra sheaf dual to
\omega_{E}
. We adopt similar notations for the dual semi-abelian scheme
E^{D}
. We let
\mathcal{H}_{E}=\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(E)
be the relative log-de Rham homology of the Kuga-Sato compactification of
E
.

Let
\operatorname{St}
be the standard representation of
G
. By construction
\operatorname{VB}_{K}^{\operatorname{can}}(\operatorname{St})=\mathcal{H}_{E}
equipped with the Gauss-Manin connection. The
\overline{B}
-filtration
0\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}((-1;1))\rightarrow\operatorname{St}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}((1;0))\rightarrow 0
gives the Hodge filtration:

0\rightarrow\omega_{E^{D}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{E}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lie}(E)\rightarrow 0.

We deduce that
\omega_{E^{D}}=\omega^{(1;0)}
and
\omega_{E}=\omega^{(1;-1)}
.

Let
\pi\colon M_{\operatorname{dR},K}\rightarrow X_{K}
be the
T
-torsor over
X_{K}
. We have
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{M_{\operatorname{dR},K}}=\oplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)}\omega^{\kappa}
, and hence we deduce that

\mathrm{H}^{0}(M_{\operatorname{dR},K},\mathcal{O}_{M_{\operatorname{dR},K}})=\bigoplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)}\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{K},\omega^{\kappa})

and that
\mathrm{Hom}_{T}(-w_{0}\kappa,\mathrm{H}^{0}(M_{\operatorname{dR},K},\mathcal{O}_{M_{\operatorname{dR},K}}))=\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{K},\omega^{\kappa})
.

A.3.2.
Nearly holomorphic modular forms

We now discuss nearly holomorphic modular forms. For an integer
r\geq 0
and
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
, let
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r}=\operatorname{VB}^{\operatorname{can}}_{K}(\mathrm{Fil}_{r}(M_{\kappa}^{\vee}))
and
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}=\operatorname{VB}^{\operatorname{can}}_{K}(M_{\kappa}^{\vee})
. We have a connection
\nabla_{\kappa}:\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X_{K}/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{log}\,D)
, where
D
denotes the boundary divisor of
X_{K}
. This connection satisfies
\nabla_{\kappa}(\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r})\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r+1}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X_{K}/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{log}\,D)
.

Remark A.3.2
.

We have
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r}=\mathrm{Sym}^{r}\mathcal{H}_{E}\otimes\omega^{\kappa-r((1;0))}
. Furthermore, we also have
\Omega^{1}_{X_{K}/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{log}\,D)\cong\omega^{2\rho}
by the Kodaira-Spencer isomorphism.

The space of nearly holomorphic modular forms of weight
\kappa
, degree
r
and level
K
is precisely the space
\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{K},\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r})
. Using the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism, we have a map

\nabla_{\kappa}:\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{K},\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{K},\mathcal{H}_{\kappa+2\rho,r+1}).

It is also possible to describe nearly holomorphic modular forms as functions on the torsor
P_{\operatorname{dR},K}
. Let
\pi^{\prime}:P_{\operatorname{dR},K}\rightarrow X_{K}
be the
\overline{B}
-torsor over
X_{K}
.

Proposition A.3.3
.

The following properties are satisfied:

(1)

The sheaf
\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}}
has an action of
\overline{B}
and the action of the unipotent radical
\overline{U}
yields a filtration
\mathrm{Fil}_{r}\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}}=\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}}[\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{r+1}]
.

(2)

The action of
T
yields a decomposition

\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}}=\bigoplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)}\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}

compatible with the filtration.

(3)

We have a connection
\nabla:\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}}\rightarrow\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{K}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{K}/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{log}\,D)
which commutes with the action of
T
,

(4)

We have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\overline{B}}(2\rho,\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR},K}})=\Omega^{1}_{X_{K}/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{log}\,D)
.

Proof.

The
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{B})
-module
\mathscr{O}_{\overline{B}}
corresponds to the tautological torsor
G\rightarrow G/\overline{B}
which corresponds to
P_{\operatorname{dR},K}
. ∎

We now pass to the limit over
K
and let
P_{\operatorname{dR}}=\lim_{K}P_{\operatorname{dR},K}
and
X=\lim_{K}X_{K}
.

Proposition A.3.4
.

The following properties are satisfied:

(1)

The space
\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{dR}})
has commuting actions of
\overline{B}
and
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

(2)

The
\overline{U}
-action on
\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}})
yields an increasing filtration

\mathrm{Fil}_{\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}})\subset\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}).

(3)

For each
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{T}(-w_{0}\kappa,\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}))=\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{H}_{\kappa})

and

\mathrm{Hom}_{T}(-w_{0}\kappa,\mathrm{Fil}_{r}\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}))=\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{H}_{\kappa,r})

(4)

We have a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times\overline{B}
-equivariant isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega^{1}_{X/\mathbb{Q}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}\{2\rho\}
, where the latter means we twist the action of
\overline{B}
by the character
2\rho
.

(5)

We have a
T\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-equivariant derivation

\nabla:\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}\{2\rho\}

which satisfies
\nabla(\mathrm{Fil}_{r}\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}))\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}_{r+1}\mathrm{H}^{0}(P_{\operatorname{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}\{2\rho\}
for all
r\geq 0
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition
A.3.3
.
∎
